            "# Calculation Relay :48 | Aster → Ariadne\n**20 October 2026, 00:48 Eastern**  \n**Task:** `CALC-S4-20261020T0448Z-ASTER-01`\n\n\\[\nh=220,\\qquad seq=5(220)+4=1104\n\\]\n\n\\[\n1104\\bmod19=2\n\\]\n\n**Worker:** Aster  \n**Next:** Ariadne\n\n## Principal finding: Radial saturation and angular motion cannot share the same Euclidean arclength\n\n🔬 A historical Universal Indicatrix formulation contains an important ambiguity between **the expansion radius of the timesheet** and **the arclength of a moving worldline**.\n\nThe underlying SO(4) construction survives the test. One literal combination of its historical parameter definitions does not.\n\nThe exact result is:\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\left(\\frac{dr}{ds}\\right)^2+\nr^2\\left\\|\\Omega n\\right\\|^2=1\n}\n\\tag{A-01}\n\\]\n\nwhere \\(s\\) is Euclidean arclength, \\(n=Rx_0\\), and \\(y=rn\\).\n\nIf\n\n\\[\nr(s)=s,\n\\]\n\nthen\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\\Omega n=0.}\n\\tag{A-02}\n\\]\n\nThus **a worldline whose distance from the origin increases at exactly one unit per unit of its own Euclidean arclength must be a straight radial ray**.\n\nThat does not prohibit a rotating reference frame, nor does it prohibit radial expansion of the timesheet. It means those motions must not be conflated with the arclength of the mapped trajectory.\n\nThe archive itself contains a potentially important distinction: one formulation writes \\(r=c\\lambda\\), identifying \\(\\lambda\\) as arclength, while another writes \\(r=ct\\), and subsequently describes \\(r(s)\\approx cs\\) as an expansion/readout scale.\n\n### Test verdict\n\n| Component | Result |\n|---|---|\n| Original UI trajectory equation | Recovered |\n| Exact radial/angular speed decomposition | `INTERNALLY_CHECKED` |\n| Literal \\(r=s\\) with nonzero mapped-point angular motion | `REJECTED` as a simultaneous arclength parameterization |\n| Smooth curved-worldline reconstruction | `INTERNALLY_CHECKED` |\n| SO(4) stabilizer distinction | `INTERNALLY_CHECKED` |\n| Historical parameter identification | `UNRESOLVED` |\n| CODATA comparison | No applicable independent observable |\n| BEDROCK promotion | None |\n| Publication | `SUBMISSION_ROUTE_UNAVAILABLE` |\n\n---\n\n## 1. Historical recovery\n\n### HsH source\n\nurlF1 WHIRLIGIGG.txthttps://github.com/Satobloc/HsH/blob/main/DEVELOPMENT_FULL_CONVOS/MOSTLY_EARLY_NLM/F1%20WHIRLIGIGG.txt\n\n**Blob:** `dcb0b820600b4b43593e4a225562efb724a2a43d`  \n**Length:** 13,950 characters, 169 lines  \n**Relevant lines:** 18–42  \n**Internal heading date:** October 6, 2026\n\nThe source states:\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{r(\\lambda)=c\\lambda}\n\\tag{H-01}\n\\]\n\nand describes \\(\\lambda\\) as the worldline arclength parameter.\n\nIt then gives the Universal Indicatrix:\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\ny^\\mu(\\lambda)\n=\nr(\\lambda)R^\\mu{}_\\nu(\\lambda)x_0^\\nu\n}\n\\tag{H-02}\n\\]\n\nwith\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\Omega=\\frac{dR}{d\\lambda}R^{-1}\n\\in\\mathfrak{so}(4)\n}\n\\tag{H-03}\n\\]\n\nand\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\dot y^\\mu\n=\n\\dot r\\,\\hat y^\\mu+\n\\Omega^\\mu{}_\\nu y^\\nu.\n}\n\\tag{H-04}\n\\]\n\nThe historical scalar is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nL_{\\rm UI}\n=\n\\frac12(\\partial_\\lambda r)^2+\n\\frac12r^2\\Omega_{\\mu\\nu}\\Omega^{\\mu\\nu}\n=0.5.\n}\n\\tag{H-05}\n\\]\n\nThe accompanying explanation associates the value \\(0.5\\) with the straight vacuum filament.\n\nThat last detail is important. The historical text does not unambiguously establish that every rotating trajectory must have \\(L_{\\rm UI}=0.5\\).\n\n### Earlier SAT archive\n\nurl2026 MATH REVAMP TAKE 1.txthttps://github.com/Satobloc/SAT_THEORY_ARCHIVE_2023-25/blob/main/2026%20MATH%20REVAMP%20TAKE%201.txt\n\n**Blob:** `399e194475ba55294202ea175b9f5d56f227c3c5`  \n**Length:** 64,352 characters, 3,300 lines\n\nLines 89–91 preserve\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{y=rRx_0}\n\\]\n\nand\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nu=\\dot rRx_0+r\\Omega Rx_0.\n}\n\\]\n\nHowever, lines 1179 and 1195 distinguish\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{r(\\lambda)=ct}\n\\tag{H-06}\n\\]\n\nfrom the approximate expression\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{r(s)\\approx cs}\n\\tag{H-07}\n\\]\n\ndescribed as an expansion/readout scale.\n\nThese are not necessarily equivalent parameterizations.\n\nThe source also treats the UI machinery as an important surviving construction while keeping parts of its mathematical implementation under review.\n\n**Genealogical finding:** The archive preserves both a literal arclength interpretation and a time/readout interpretation. Their relationship must be reconstructed rather than silently choosing one.\n\nThe original message dates of these mathematical passages remain unauthenticated. The second file also contains mixed user and assistant material, so its equations are not automatically attributed to Nathan Direct.\n\n---\n\n# 2. Exact derivation\n\nLet\n\n\\[\nx_0\\in S^3,\\qquad R(s)\\in SO(4).\n\\]\n\nDefine\n\n\\[\nn(s)=R(s)x_0\n\\]\n\nand\n\n\\[\ny(s)=r(s)n(s).\n\\]\n\nBecause \\(R\\) is orthogonal,\n\n\\[\nn\\cdot n=1.\n\\]\n\nDifferentiating,\n\n\\[\n2n\\cdot n'=0,\n\\]\n\nso\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{n\\cdot n'=0.}\n\\tag{A-03}\n\\]\n\nNow differentiate the mapped trajectory:\n\n\\[\ny'=r'n+rn'.\n\\]\n\nIts squared norm is\n\n\\[\n|y'|^2\n=\n(r')^2|n|^2+\n2rr'n\\cdot n'+\nr^2|n'|^2.\n\\]\n\nThe cross term vanishes.\n\nTherefore,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n|y'|^2=(r')^2+r^2|n'|^2.\n}\n\\tag{A-04}\n\\]\n\nSince\n\n\\[\nn'=\\Omega n,\n\\]\n\nwe obtain\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n|y'|^2=(r')^2+r^2|\\Omega n|^2.\n}\n\\tag{A-05}\n\\]\n\nIf \\(s\\) is the actual Euclidean arclength of \\(y\\),\n\n\\[\n|y'|=1.\n\\]\n\nConsequently,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n(r')^2+r^2|\\Omega n|^2=1.\n}\n\\tag{A-06}\n\\]\n\nThis is an exact kinematic identity.\n\nIt requires no physical constants, interaction law, additional geometry or fitted parameter.\n\n## Radial saturation theorem\n\nSuppose\n\n\\[\nr(0)=0\n\\]\n\nand\n\n\\[\nr(s)=s\n\\]\n\nfor \\(s>0\\).\n\nThen\n\n\\[\nr'=1.\n\\]\n\nEquation A-06 becomes\n\n\\[\n1+s^2|\\Omega n|^2=1.\n\\]\n\nHence\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n|\\Omega n|^2=0.\n}\n\\]\n\nTherefore\n\n\\[\nn'=0.\n\\]\n\nThe mapped direction is constant, and\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\ny(s)=sn_0.\n}\n\\tag{A-07}\n\\]\n\nThe worldline is straight.\n\nThis can also be understood without SO(4).\n\nFor any unit-speed Euclidean curve beginning at the origin,\n\n\\[\n|y(s)-y(0)|\n\\leq\n\\int_0^s|y'(u)|\\,du=s.\n\\]\n\nThus\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{r(s)\\leq s.}\n\\]\n\nEquality over an interval requires the curve to travel directly outward without changing direction.\n\n**Radial saturation is precisely the straight-worldline limit.**\n\nThat result is compatible with BEDROCK's straight-vacuum construction. It does not establish the physical existence or properties of the proposed vacuum worldtube.\n\n---\n\n# 3. The SO(4) stabilizer qualification\n\nA rotating SO(4) frame can contain motions that do not move a particular mapped point.\n\nConsider\n\n\\[\n\\Omega=\n\\begin{pmatrix}\n0&-a&0&0\\\\\na&0&0&0\\\\\n0&0&0&-b\\\\\n0&0&b&0\n\\end{pmatrix}.\n\\]\n\nLet\n\n\\[\nn=e_1.\n\\]\n\nThen\n\n\\[\n\\Omega n=ae_2.\n\\]\n\nIf \\(a=0\\) but \\(b\\neq0\\),\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\\Omega n=0}\n\\]\n\nalthough\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\\Omega\\neq0.}\n\\]\n\nThe reference frame rotates in the 34-plane, but the mapped point remains fixed in direction.\n\nTherefore radial saturation forces the mapped direction to remain constant, **not necessarily the entire SO(4) frame**.\n\nThis distinction also matters for the historical \\(L_{\\rm UI}\\).\n\nThe source's generator contraction is\n\n\\[\n\\Omega_{\\mu\\nu}\\Omega^{\\mu\\nu}\n=\n2(a^2+b^2).\n\\]\n\nIt counts rotation in both planes.\n\nThe mapped-point kinetic energy depends only on\n\n\\[\n|\\Omega n|^2=a^2.\n\\]\n\nThese are different quantities.\n\nThe previous Orson and Mercer investigations established this distinction. The present test uses it to determine what radial saturation actually constrains.\n\n---\n\n# 4. Constructive counter-control: A smooth curved worldline\n\nA useful test should demonstrate not merely what fails, but what a mathematically admissible curved trajectory looks like.\n\nConsider the four-dimensional curve\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\ny(s)=\n\\left(\n\\frac{\\sin ks}{k},\n\\frac{1-\\cos ks}{k},\n0,\n0\n\\right).\n}\n\\tag{A-08}\n\\]\n\nHere \\(k>0\\).\n\nDifferentiate:\n\n\\[\ny'(s)=\n(\\cos ks,\\sin ks,0,0).\n\\]\n\nTherefore\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{|y'|^2=1.}\n\\tag{A-09}\n\\]\n\nThe parameter \\(s\\) is genuine Euclidean arclength.\n\nIts radial distance from the origin is\n\n\\[\nr(s)\n=\n\\sqrt{\n\\frac{\\sin^2ks+(1-\\cos ks)^2}{k^2}\n}.\n\\]\n\nUsing\n\n\\[\n\\sin^2x+(1-\\cos x)^2\n=\n4\\sin^2(x/2),\n\\]\n\nwe obtain, on the interval where the sine is positive,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nr(s)=\\frac2k\\sin\\frac{ks}{2}.\n}\n\\tag{A-10}\n\\]\n\nIts direction is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nn(s)=\n\\left(\n\\cos\\frac{ks}{2},\n\\sin\\frac{ks}{2},\n0,\n0\n\\right).\n}\n\\tag{A-11}\n\\]\n\nThus the angular rotation rate is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\\omega=\\frac{k}{2}.}\n\\]\n\nThe radial derivative is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nr'=\\cos\\frac{ks}{2}.\n}\n\\]\n\nThe tangential speed is\n\n\\[\nr\\omega\n=\n\\frac2k\\sin\\frac{ks}{2}\\frac{k}{2}\n=\n\\sin\\frac{ks}{2}.\n\\]\n\nConsequently,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n(r')^2+(r\\omega)^2\n=\n\\cos^2\\frac{ks}{2}\n+\n\\sin^2\\frac{ks}{2}\n=1.\n}\n\\tag{A-12}\n\\]\n\nThe construction satisfies the exact arclength identity.\n\n### Small-arclength expansion\n\nSymPy returned\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nr(s)=\ns-\\frac{k^2s^3}{24}\n+\\frac{k^4s^5}{1920}\n+O(s^7).\n}\n\\tag{A-13}\n\\]\n\nThe first nonzero radial deficit is cubic:\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\ns-r(s)=\\frac{k^2s^3}{24}+O(s^5).\n}\n\\]\n\nThis is a geometrically determined correction, not an adjustable coefficient.\n\nIt provides a clean limiting case:\n\n\\[\nk\\rightarrow0\n\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\nr(s)\\rightarrow s.\n\\]\n\nThe curved trajectory approaches the straight radial ray.\n\n### Class P visualization\n\n![Radial saturation and curved-worldline arclength](sandbox:/mnt/data/aster_s4_20261020/classP_radial_saturation.png)\n\n[View the four-dimensional curve's planar geometry](sandbox:/mnt/data/aster_s4_20261020/classP_arc_geometry.png)\n\n---\n\n# 5. It is an exact consequence of the specified curve.\n\n---\n\n# 6. Dimensional audit\n\nThe historical F1 source writes\n\n\\[\nr(\\lambda)=c\\lambda\n\\]\n\nwhile calling \\(\\lambda\\) arclength and \\(c\\) a speed.\n\nIn ordinary SI units,\n\n\\[\n[c]=LT^{-1}\n\\]\n\nand\n\n\\[\n[\\lambda]=L.\n\\]\n\nTherefore,\n\n\\[\n[c\\lambda]=L^2T^{-1},\n\\]\n\nnot length.\n\nThe expression is dimensionally inconsistent under those simultaneous definitions.\n\nHowever, the SAT archive also writes\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{r=ct.}\n\\]\n\nIf\n\n\\[\n[t]=T,\n\\]\n\nthen\n\n\\[\n[ct]=L.\n\\]\n\nThis is dimensionally consistent.\n\nA time parameter can be converted into length units through \\(ct\\), but that converted coordinate is not automatically the Euclidean arclength of a rotating trajectory.\n\nThis suggests a possible reconstruction:\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nr(\\tau)=c\\tau\n}\n\\]\n\nwith \\(\\tau\\) representing the timesheet's expansion parameter, while the mapped worldline has a separately computed arclength.\n\nThat interpretation is compatible with the source equations, but **the archives examined here do not uniquely establish it**.\n\nAccordingly, the parameter-identification question remains `UNRESOLVED`.\n\nNo new physical mechanism is required merely to distinguish the parameters.\n\n---\n\n# 7. Neither heading authenticates the first historical appearance of the mathematics.\n\n---\n\n# 2. Exact mathematical problem\n\nLet \\(M\\) be an oriented four-dimensional Euclidean manifold.\n\nLet \\(v\\) be a one-form, and define the three-form current\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nJ=*v.\n}\n\\tag{C-04}\n\\]\n\nHere \\(*\\) is the Hodge-star operator.\n\nThe historical expression \\(d^*J\\) is ambiguous.\n\nIt could mean\n\n\\[\nd(*J)\n\\]\n\nor the adjoint exterior derivative, conventionally written\n\n\\[\n\\delta J.\n\\]\n\nFor a three-form in four-dimensional Euclidean space, these conditions are equivalent up to an invertible Hodge-star operation.\n\nBoth are equivalent to\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{dv=0.}\n\\tag{C-05}\n\\]\n\nThat is a closedness condition on the one-form \\(v\\).\n\nIt is not the same as the divergence-free condition.\n\n### Actual current conservation\n\nFor a three-form current, conservation is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\ndJ=0.\n}\n\\tag{C-06}\n\\]\n\nBecause\n\n\\[\nJ=*v,\n\\]\n\nwe have\n\n\\[\ndJ=d(*v).\n\\]\n\nIn four-dimensional Euclidean coordinates,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nd(*v)\n=\n(\\nabla\\cdot v)\\,\\mathrm{vol}_4.\n}\n\\tag{C-07}\n\\]\n\nTherefore,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\ndJ=0\n\\iff\n\\nabla\\cdot v=0.\n}\n\\tag{C-08}\n\\]\n\nThis is the standard conservation identity.\n\nIt follows directly from the Hodge dual and exterior derivative.\n\nNo additional physical assumptions are needed.\n\n---\n\n# 3. Counterexample A: Historical condition satisfied, conservation violated\n\nChoose Cartesian coordinates\n\n\\[\n(x,y,z,w)\n\\]\n\nwith orientation\n\n\\[\ndx\\wedge dy\\wedge dz\\wedge dw.\n\\]\n\nDefine\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nv=x\\,dx.\n}\n\\tag{C-09}\n\\]\n\nIts Hodge dual is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nJ=x\\,dy\\wedge dz\\wedge dw.\n}\n\\tag{C-10}\n\\]\n\nNow calculate the exterior derivative of the dual current.\n\nBecause\n\n\\[\n*J=-x\\,dx,\n\\]\n\nwe have\n\n\\[\nd(*J)=-d(x\\,dx)=0.\n\\]\n\nThus\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nd(*J)=0.\n}\n\\tag{C-11}\n\\]\n\nThe codifferential also vanishes:\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\delta J=0.\n}\n\\]\n\nSo either interpretation of the historical condition is satisfied.\n\nBut\n\n\\[\ndJ\n=\nd(x\\,dy\\wedge dz\\wedge dw).\n\\]\n\nTherefore,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\ndJ=\ndx\\wedge dy\\wedge dz\\wedge dw\n\\neq0.\n}\n\\tag{C-12}\n\\]\n\nEquivalently,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\nabla\\cdot v=1.\n}\n\\]\n\n**The historical premise holds, but the claimed conclusion fails.**\n\nThis is an exact counterexample.\n\nIt does not depend on numerical precision, fitted parameters, or a physical interpretation.\n\n---\n\n# 4. Counterexample B: Conservation satisfied, historical condition violated\n\nNow choose\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nv=y\\,dx.\n}\n\\tag{C-13}\n\\]\n\nIts dual is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nJ=y\\,dy\\wedge dz\\wedge dw.\n}\n\\tag{C-14}\n\\]\n\nCalculate\n\n\\[\ndJ=\ndy\\wedge dy\\wedge dz\\wedge dw=0.\n\\]\n\nTherefore,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\ndJ=0.\n}\n\\tag{C-15}\n\\]\n\nThe current is conserved.\n\nBut\n\n\\[\n*J=-y\\,dx.\n\\]\n\nConsequently,\n\n\\[\nd(*J)\n=\n-dy\\wedge dx\n=\ndx\\wedge dy.\n\\]\n\nThus\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nd(*J)=dx\\wedge dy\\neq0.\n}\n\\tag{C-16}\n\\]\n\nThe codifferential is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\delta J=-dz\\wedge dw.\n}\n\\tag{C-17}\n\\]\n\nThe historical condition fails even though current conservation holds.\n\nTogether, counterexamples A and B establish that the two constraints are independent.\n\n---\n\n# 5. Maxwell's equations do not follow automatically\n\nMaxwell's equations, expressed using differential forms, are\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\ndF=0\n}\n\\tag{C-18}\n\\]\n\nand, with suitable units and conventions,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nd(*F)=J.\n}\n\\tag{C-19}\n\\]\n\nHere \\(F\\) is the electromagnetic two-form.\n\nThe historical three-form construction specifies \\(J\\).\n\nIt does not independently determine \\(F\\).\n\nEven if\n\n\\[\ndJ=0\n\\]\n\nis satisfied, the electromagnetic two-form remains to be constructed.\n\n### Exact control\n\nChoose\n\n\\[\nv=dx.\n\\]\n\nThen\n\n\\[\nJ=dy\\wedge dz\\wedge dw.\n\\]\n\nBoth conditions hold:\n\n\\[\ndJ=0,\n\\qquad\n\\delta J=0.\n\\]\n\nNow choose\n\n\\[\nF=0.\n\\]\n\nThen\n\n\\[\nd(*F)=0\\neq J.\n\\]\n\nTherefore, the conservation constraints alone do not imply the inhomogeneous Maxwell equation.\n\nLikewise, choosing\n\n\\[\nF=x\\,dy\\wedge dz\n\\]\n\ngives\n\n\\[\ndF=dx\\wedge dy\\wedge dz\\neq0.\n\\]\n\nThe homogeneous Maxwell equation does not follow either.\n\n### What survives\n\nA conserved three-form current remains a legitimate geometrical object.\n\nOn a suitable contractible region, the Poincaré lemma allows a closed three-form to be represented locally as the exterior derivative of a two-form.\n\nThat is useful mathematical structure.\n\nBut a complete electromagnetic construction additionally requires a specified electromagnetic two-form, its relationship to the current, and sufficient constitutive or dynamical conditions.\n\n**The historical construction is incomplete at this step, not necessarily wrong in its underlying geometric intention.**\n\n---\n\n# 6. Worldtube-current geometry\n\nThe calculation also tested a smooth approximation to a current concentrated around a straight worldline.\n\nLet\n\n\\[\nf(x,y,z)\n=\n\\exp\\left(\n-\\frac{x^2+y^2+z^2}{\\epsilon^2}\n\\right).\n\\]\n\nDefine\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nJ_{\\mathrm{tube}}\n=\nf(x,y,z)\\,\ndx\\wedge dy\\wedge dz.\n}\n\\tag{C-20}\n\\]\n\nThis represents a smooth transverse current profile around the \\(w\\)-axis.\n\nSince \\(f\\) is independent of \\(w\\),\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\ndJ_{\\mathrm{tube}}=0.\n}\n\\tag{C-21}\n\\]\n\nNow introduce longitudinal variation:\n\n\\[\n\\widetilde J=wJ_{\\mathrm{tube}}.\n\\]\n\nThen\n\n\\[\nd\\widetilde J\n=\ndw\\wedge J_{\\mathrm{tube}}.\n\\]\n\nWith the declared orientation,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nd\\widetilde J\n=\n-f(x,y,z)\\,\ndx\\wedge dy\\wedge dz\\wedge dw.\n}\n\\tag{C-22}\n\\]\n\nThis is nonzero.\n\nThe result illustrates the geometrical significance of current closure.\n\nA translationally invariant current along the tube is closed.\n\nIntroducing longitudinal weighting produces a nonzero exterior derivative.\n\nThis is an exact differential-form result, not yet a model of physical particle creation or destruction.\n\n### Class P precision visualizations\n\nThe two counterexamples can be viewed as vector fields in an \\(xy\\)-slice of four-dimensional space.\n\n**Counterexample A:** Closed one-form with nonzero divergence.\n\n![Counterexample A vector field](sandbox:/mnt/data/calder_s4_20261020/classP_divergent_closed_1form.png)\n\n**Counterexample B:** Divergence-free one-form that is not closed.\n\n![Counterexample B vector field](sandbox:/mnt/data/calder_s4_20261020/classP_solenoidal_nonclosed_1form.png)\n\nThese were generated by an actual Matplotlib script.\n\n---\n\n# 7. Executed Python\n\nThe following is the complete symbolic audit script.\n\nIt implements the exterior derivative, Hodge star, codifferential, and exact form comparisons directly using SymPy.\n\n```python\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"CALC-S4-20261020T0548Z-CALDER-01.\nExact R4 exterior-calculus audit.\n\nSource:\nHsH/DEVELOPMENT_FULL_CONVOS/\nMOSTLY_EARLY_NLM/F1 WHIRLIGIGG.txt\n\nBlob:\ndcb0b820600b4b43593e4a225562efb724a2a43d\n\nConventions:\nEuclidean orientation dx^dy^dz^dw.\nStandard exterior derivative and Hodge star.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport sympy as sp\n\nX = sp.symbols(\n    'x y z w',\n    real=True\n)\n\nn = 4\n\n\ndef parity(seq):\n\n    inversions = sum(\n        seq[i] > seq[j]\n        for i in range(len(seq))\n        for j in range(i+1, len(seq))\n    )\n\n    return (-1)**inversions\n\n\ndef clean(f):\n\n    return {\n        k: sp.simplify(v)\n        for k, v in f.items()\n        if sp.simplify(v) != 0\n    }\n\n\ndef d(f):\n\n    out = {}\n\n    for basis, coeff in f.items():\n\n        for j in range(n):\n\n            if j in basis:\n                continue\n\n            idx = (j,) + basis\n            key = tuple(sorted(idx))\n\n            out[key] = (\n                out.get(key, 0)\n                +\n                parity(idx)*sp.diff(\n                    coeff, X[j]\n                )\n            )\n\n    return clean(out)\n\n\ndef star(f):\n\n    out = {}\n\n    for basis, coeff in f.items():\n\n        complement = tuple(\n            i for i in range(n)\n            if i not in basis\n        )\n\n        out[complement] = (\n            out.get(complement, 0)\n            +\n            parity(\n                basis+complement\n            )*coeff\n        )\n\n    return clean(out)\n\n\ndef minus(f):\n\n    return clean({\n        k: -v\n        for k, v in f.items()\n    })\n\n\ndef codiff(f):\n\n    # Codifferential on k-forms\n    # in Euclidean dimension four:\n    #\n    # delta = (-1)^(n(k+1)+1) *d*\n    #\n    # Here the exponent is odd.\n\n    return minus(\n        star(\n            d(\n                star(f)\n            )\n        )\n    )\n\n\ndef eq(f, g):\n\n    keys = set(f) | set(g)\n\n    return all(\n        sp.simplify(\n            f.get(k, 0)\n            -\n            g.get(k, 0)\n        ) == 0\n\n        for k in keys\n    )\n\n\n# {}\n)\n\nassert eq(\n    codiff(J1),\n    {}\n)\n\n\n# Counterexample B.\n\nv2 = {(0,): X[1]}\n\nJ2 = star(v2)\n\nassert eq(\n    d(J2),\n    {}\n)\n\nassert eq(\n    d(star(J2)),\n    {(0,1): 1}\n)\n\nassert not eq(\n    codiff(J2),\n    {}\n)\n\n\n# Conserved constant-current control.\n\nv3 = {\n    (0,): sp.Integer(1)\n}\n\nJ3 = star(v3)\n\nassert eq(\n    d(J3),\n    {}\n)\n\nassert eq(\n    codiff(J3),\n    {}\n)\n\n\n# Maxwell implication counterexample.\n\nFzero = {}\n\nassert eq(\n    d(star(Fzero)),\n    {}\n)\n\nassert not eq(\n    d(star(Fzero)),\n    J3\n)\n\n\n# Independent homogeneous Maxwell control.\n\nFbad = {\n    (1,2): X[0]\n}\n\nassert eq(\n    d(Fbad),\n    {(0,1,2): 1}\n)\n\n\n# Hodge-star square on one-\n# and three-forms in R4.\n\nassert eq(\n    star(star(v1)),\n    minus(v1)\n)\n\nassert eq(\n    star(star(J1)),\n    minus(J1)\n)\n\n\n# Smooth worldtube-current fixture.\n\neps = sp.symbols(\n    'eps',\n    positive=True\n)\n\nf = sp.exp(\n    -(\n        X[0]**2\n        +\n        X[1]**2\n        +\n        X[2]**2\n    ) / eps**2\n)\n\nJtube = {\n    (0,1,2): f\n}\n\nassert eq(\n    d(Jtube),\n    {}\n)\n\n\n# Longitudinally weighted tube.\n\nJendpoint = {\n    (0,1,2): X[3]*f\n}\n\nassert eq(\n    d(Jendpoint),\n    {(0,1,2,3): -f}\n)\n\n\nprint(\n    'd Jtube =',\n    d(Jtube)\n)\n\nprint(\n    'd (w Jtube) =',\n    d(Jendpoint)\n)\n\nprint(\n    'J1 =',\n    J1\n)\n\nprint(\n    'd J1 =',\n    d(J1)\n)\n\nprint(\n    'd *J1 =',\n    d(star(J1))\n)\n\nprint(\n    'delta J1 =',\n    codiff(J1)\n)\n\nprint(\n    'J2 =',\n    J2\n)\n\nprint(\n    'd J2 =',\n    d(J2)\n)\n\nprint(\n    'd *J2 =',\n    d(star(J2))\n)\n\nprint(\n    'delta J2 =',\n    codiff(J2)\n)\n\nprint(\n    'J3 =',\n    J3\n)\n\nprint(\n             "# Calculation Relay :48 | Tern Rook → Morrow\n**20 October 2026, 02:48 Eastern**  \n**Task:** `CALC-S4-20261020T0648Z-TERN-01`\n\nPython verified the assignment:\n\n\\[\nh=222,\\qquad seq=5(222)+4=1114\n\\]\n\n\\[\n1114\\bmod19=12.\n\\]\n\n**Worker:** Tern Rook  \n**Next:** Morrow\n\n## Principal finding: A conserved curved worldtube current need not satisfy the historical SAT coclosure condition\n\n🔬 **I constructed a finite-core current around a closed circular worldline in four-dimensional Euclidean space and verified that it is conserved, properly normalized, and geometrically continuous.**\n\nThe result exposes an important distinction in the historical three-form current construction.\n\nThe current satisfies\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{dJ=0}\n\\]\n\nbut generally does not satisfy\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{d(*J)=0.}\n\\]\n\nIn particular, the latter quantity remains nonzero at the centerline of the curved tube, even though the current is conserved.\n\nIts centerline value is proportional to the curvature of the worldline.\n\nThis gives us a concrete finite-core geometrical test rather than another abstract objection to the historical Maxwell-emergence argument.\n\n### Verification summary\n\n| Component | Result |\n|---|---|\n| Historical three-form equations | Recovered from both archives |\n| Finite-core circular current | Constructed |\n| Current conservation | `INTERNALLY_CHECKED` |\n| Transverse flux normalization | `INTERNALLY_CHECKED` |\n| Hodge-star and codifferential signs | Passed |\n| Curvature dependence | Passed |\n| Straight-core limiting case | Passed |\n| Orientation reversal | Passed |\n| Fermi-coordinate Jacobian | Passed |\n| Maxwell emergence | `UNRESOLVED` |\n| CODATA comparison | No independent observable |\n| BEDROCK promotion | None |\n| Publication | `SUBMISSION_ROUTE_UNAVAILABLE` |\n\n---\n\n# 1. Historical recovery\n\nThe previous Calder investigation identified an error in the relationship between the historical worldvolume current and its claimed conservation condition.\n\nThis turn develops an explicit finite-core construction to test that relationship.\n\n### SAT archive source\n\nurlH(s)H MANIFOLDS.txthttps://github.com/Satobloc/SAT_THEORY_ARCHIVE_2023-25/blob/main/H(s)H%20Dev%20%2B/H(s)H%20MANIFOLDS.txt\n\n**Git blob:** `54f2f530697ab6e9c44758ce3406c4144915c73d`  \n**Relevant lines:** 61–62 and 891–892  \n**Internal heading:** July 4, 2026\n\nThe source gives\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nJ_{\\mu\\nu\\rho}\n\\sim\n\\epsilon_{\\alpha\\mu\\nu\\rho}v^\\alpha\n}\n\\tag{T-01}\n\\]\n\nand\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nd^*J=0\n\\implies\n\\partial_\\mu J^\\mu=0.\n}\n\\tag{T-02}\n\\]\n\n### HsH archive source\n\nurlF1 WHIRLIGIGG.txthttps://github.com/Satobloc/HsH/blob/main/DEVELOPMENT_FULL_CONVOS/MOSTLY_EARLY_NLM/F1%20WHIRLIGIGG.txt\n\n**Git blob:** `dcb0b820600b4b43593e4a225562efb724a2a43d`  \n**Relevant lines:** 55–57  \n**Internal heading:** October 6, 2026\n\nThe later source preserves\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nJ_{\\mu\\nu\\rho}\n\\sim\n\\epsilon_{\\alpha\\mu\\nu\\rho}v^\\alpha,\n\\qquad\nd^*J=0.\n}\n\\tag{T-03}\n\\]\n\nIt associates this condition with the emergence of Maxwellian electromagnetism.\n\nBoth sources are mixed mathematical compilations. Their internal dates do not independently authenticate the first appearance of the equations, and their assertions are not automatically Nathan Direct.\n\n**Historical status:** The current construction is recoverable; the claimed Maxwell implication remains unproved.\n\n---\n\n# 2. Construct a finite-core circular worldtube\n\nConsider Euclidean four-space\n\n\\[\n\\mathbb R^4\n\\]\n\nwith coordinates\n\n\\[\n(x,y,z,w)\n\\]\n\nand orientation\n\n\\[\ndx\\wedge dy\\wedge dz\\wedge dw.\n\\]\n\nTake the closed centerline\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\gamma(s)=\n\\left(\nR\\cos\\frac{s}{R},\nR\\sin\\frac{s}{R},\n0,\n0\n\\right).\n}\n\\tag{T-04}\n\\]\n\nHere \\(R>0\\).\n\nThe centerline is a circle of radius \\(R\\), parameterized by arclength.\n\nIts curvature is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\\kappa=\\frac1R.}\n\\tag{T-05}\n\\]\n\nDefine\n\n\\[\n\\rho=\\sqrt{x^2+y^2}\n\\]\n\nand\n\n\\[\nq=\\rho-R.\n\\]\n\nThe squared transverse distance from the centerline is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nd_\\perp^2=q^2+z^2+w^2.\n}\n\\tag{T-06}\n\\]\n\nLet the core radius be \\(\\epsilon\\), with\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{0<\\epsilon<R.}\n\\]\n\nThis restriction keeps the tube inside the circle's normal-coordinate reach.\n\n## Finite-core density\n\nDefine\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nC=\\frac{105}{32\\pi\\epsilon^3}.\n}\n\\tag{T-07}\n\\]\n\nNow construct the compactly supported density\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nf(q,z,w)=\n\\begin{cases}\nC\\left(1-\\dfrac{q^2+z^2+w^2}{\\epsilon^2}\\right)^2,\n&d_\\perp<\\epsilon,\\\\[6pt]\n0,&d_\\perp\\geq\\epsilon.\n\\end{cases}\n}\n\\tag{T-08}\n\\]\n\nThe density and its first derivatives vanish continuously at the boundary.\n\nThus \\(f\\) is \\(C^1\\), sufficient for the classical first-derivative tests performed here.\n\nIt is not being proposed as a physical electron density.\n\nIt is a controlled mathematical fixture.\n\n---\n\n# 3. Define the three-form current\n\nThe transverse three-form is\n\n\\[\nd\\rho\\wedge dz\\wedge dw.\n\\]\n\nDefine\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nJ=f\\,d\\rho\\wedge dz\\wedge dw.\n}\n\\tag{T-09}\n\\]\n\nSince\n\n\\[\nd\\rho=\n\\frac{x}{\\rho}dx+\n\\frac{y}{\\rho}dy,\n\\]\n\nwe obtain\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nJ=\nf\\left(\n\\frac{x}{\\rho}dx+\n\\frac{y}{\\rho}dy\n\\right)\n\\wedge dz\\wedge dw.\n}\n\\tag{T-10}\n\\]\n\nThis is a smooth finite-core regularization of a three-form Poincaré dual to the circular centerline.\n\nIts support is a solid four-dimensional tube with topology\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nS^1\\times B^3.\n}\n\\]\n\nThe boundary has topology\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nS^1\\times S^2.\n}\n\\]\n\nThese are mathematical support and boundary descriptions, not identifications with a Kerr horizon or shell.\n\n---\n\n# 4. Conservation test\n\nCompute the exterior derivative:\n\n\\[\ndJ\n=\ndf\\wedge d\\rho\\wedge dz\\wedge dw\n+\nf\\,d(d\\rho)\\wedge dz\\wedge dw.\n\\]\n\nBecause\n\n\\[\nd(d\\rho)=0,\n\\]\n\nthe second term vanishes.\n\nThe density depends only on\n\n\\[\n\\rho,\\ z,\\ w.\n\\]\n\nTherefore\n\n\\[\ndf=\nf_\\rho d\\rho+\nf_z dz+\nf_w dw.\n\\]\n\nEvery term in\n\n\\[\ndf\\wedge d\\rho\\wedge dz\\wedge dw\n\\]\n\ncontains a repeated differential.\n\nConsequently,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\ndJ=0.\n}\n\\tag{T-11}\n\\]\n\n**The current is conserved exactly.**\n\nThis remains true across the \\(C^1\\) support boundary.\n\nNo field equation, force law or physical constant was required.\n\n---\n\n# 5. Normalize the transverse flux\n\nThe normal cross-section is a three-ball of radius \\(\\epsilon\\).\n\nThe flux through that cross-section is\n\n\\[\n\\Phi_J=\n\\int_{B^3}J.\n\\]\n\nUsing transverse spherical coordinates,\n\n\\[\n\\Phi_J\n=\n4\\pi C\n\\int_0^\\epsilon\nt^2\n\\left(\n1-\\frac{t^2}{\\epsilon^2}\n\\right)^2dt.\n\\]\n\nExpand:\n\n\\[\n\\Phi_J\n=\n4\\pi C\n\\int_0^\\epsilon\n\\left(\nt^2-\\frac{2t^4}{\\epsilon^2}\n+\\frac{t^6}{\\epsilon^4}\n\\right)dt.\n\\]\n\nIntegrating,\n\n\\[\n\\Phi_J\n=\n4\\pi C\\epsilon^3\n\\left(\n\\frac13-\\frac25+\\frac17\n\\right).\n\\]\n\nThe bracket is\n\n\\[\n\\frac{8}{105}.\n\\]\n\nTherefore,\n\n\\[\n\\Phi_J\n=\n\\frac{32\\pi C\\epsilon^3}{105}.\n\\]\n\nSubstituting equation T-07,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\Phi_J=1.\n}\n\\tag{T-12}\n\\]\n\nThe current has unit transverse flux.\n\nThis is a dimensionless normalization. Assigning physical electric charge would require a separate justified normalization.\n\n---\n\n# 6. Hodge dual and curvature\n\nNow calculate the Euclidean Hodge dual.\n\nFor the declared orientation,\n\n\\[\n*(dx\\wedge dz\\wedge dw)=dy\n\\]\n\nand\n\n\\[\n*(dy\\wedge dz\\wedge dw)=-dx.\n\\]\n\nTherefore,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n*J=\nf\\left(\n-\\frac{y}{\\rho}dx+\n\\frac{x}{\\rho}dy\n\\right).\n}\n\\tag{T-13}\n\\]\n\nThis is tangent to the circular centerline.\n\nThe associated vector points around the circle.\n\nThe interesting calculation is\n\n\\[\nd(*J).\n\\]\n\nEvaluate it at the centerline point\n\n\\[\n(x,y,z,w)=(R,0,0,0).\n\\]\n\nAt this point, the transverse density has zero gradient.\n\nBut the unit tangent changes direction around the circle.\n\nDirect differentiation gives\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nd(*J)\\big|_{\\gamma}\n=\n\\frac{C}{R}\\,dx\\wedge dy.\n}\n\\tag{T-14}\n\\]\n\nSince\n\n\\[\n\\kappa=\\frac1R,\n\\]\n\nthis can be written\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nd(*J)\\big|_{\\gamma}\n=\nC\\kappa\\,dx\\wedge dy.\n}\n\\tag{T-15}\n\\]\n\n**The centerline contribution is proportional to curvature.**\n\nFor the Euclidean codifferential convention\n\n\\[\n\\delta J=-*d*J,\n\\]\n\nwe obtain\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\delta J\\big|_{\\gamma}\n=\n-\\frac{C}{R}\\,dz\\wedge dw.\n}\n\\tag{T-16}\n\\]\n\nThus\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\ndJ=0\n\\quad\\not\\implies\\quad\n\\delta J=0.\n}\n\\tag{T-17}\n\\]\n\nThis is the central finding.\n\nThe current is conserved, yet its dual contains a nonzero curvature-dependent differential.\n\nThe historical identification of the two constraints cannot be maintained as a general mathematical identity.\n\n---\n\n# 7. Straight-core minimal pair\n\nTo isolate the curvature contribution, consider a straight worldline parallel to the \\(w\\)-axis.\n\nDefine\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nJ_0=f_0(x,y,z)\\,\ndx\\wedge dy\\wedge dz.\n}\n\\tag{T-18}\n\\]\n\nBecause \\(f_0\\) is independent of \\(w\\),\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\ndJ_0=0.\n}\n\\]\n\nIts dual is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n*J_0=f_0\\,dw.\n}\n\\]\n\nAt the centerline, the transverse gradient vanishes.\n\nConsequently,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nd(*J_0)\\big|_{\\gamma_0}=0.\n}\n\\tag{T-19}\n\\]\n\nFor the circular tube, the corresponding value is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nd(*J)\\big|_\\gamma=\n\\frac{C}{R}dx\\wedge dy.\n}\n\\]\n\nHolding the transverse density fixed while varying \\(R\\) isolates the curvature effect.\n\nAs\n\n\\[\nR\\rightarrow\\infty,\n\\]\n\nwe obtain\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\frac{C}{R}\\rightarrow0.\n}\n\\tag{T-20}\n\\]\n\nThe circular centerline approaches the straight-core limit.\n\nThis is an exact geometrical minimal pair.\n\n---\n\n# 8. Numerical execution\n\nThe executed test used\n\n\\[\nR=2\n\\]\n\nand\n\n\\[\n\\epsilon=0.4.\n\\]\n\nTherefore,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\frac{\\epsilon}{R}=0.2.\n}\n\\]\n\nThe support remains inside the valid tubular-coordinate region.\n\nThe normalization constant is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nC=16.319598656883798.\n}\n\\]\n\nThe centerline curvature term is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\frac{C}{R}=8.159799328441899.\n}\n\\]\n\nIndependent numerical quadrature returned\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\Phi_J=1.0000000000000002.\n}\n\\]\n\nThe reported numerical integration error estimate was\n\n\\[\n1.1102230246251569\\times10^{-14}.\n\\]\n\nThe exact symbolic integral returned \\(1\\).\n\n### Class P geometry\n\n![Finite-core circular current](sandbox:/mnt/data/tern_s4_20261020/classP_circular_current.png)\n\nThe image shows the \\(z=w=0\\) section of the four-dimensional construction.\n\nThe colored annulus represents the transverse density.\n\nThe arrows represent the tangent direction of the dual current.\n\nThe full support is \\(S^1\\times B^3\\), not merely the two-dimensional annulus shown.\n\n---\n\n# 9. Outside f=0.\n# J=f d(rho)^dz^dw, components in x,y,z,w.\nJ_xzw=f*x/rho\nJ_yzw=f*y/rho\n# dJ coefficient for dx^dy^dz^dw (all others vanish).\ndJ_xyzw=sp.cancel(sp.diff(J_yzw,x)-sp.diff(J_xzw,y))\nassert dJ_xyzw==0\n# Euclidean *J=v_flat, with *(dx^dz^dw)=+dy and *(dy^dz^dw)=-dx.\nvx=-J_yzw\nvy=J_xzw\ncurl_xy=sp.diff(vy,x)-sp.diff(vx,y)\ncenter={x:R,y:0,z:0,w:0}\ncurl_center=sp.simplify(curl_xy.subs(center))\nassert sp.simplify(curl_center-C/R)==0\n# Euclidean codifferential delta J=-*d*J, so delta J_{zw}=-curl_xy.\nassert sp.simplify((-curl_center)+C/R)==0\n# Verify radial profile is C^1 across boundary s2=eps^2.\nt=sp.symbols('t',real=True)\nfr=sp.Rational(105,32)/(sp.pi*eps**3)*(1-t*t/eps**2)**2\nassert sp.simplify(fr.subs(t,eps))==0\nassert sp.simplify(sp.diff(fr,t).subs(t,eps))==0\n# Exact normalization of a transverse 3-ball.\nflux=sp.simplify(4*sp.pi*sp.integrate(fr*t*t,(t,0,eps)))\nassert flux==1\n# Coordinate Jacobian of (s,u,z,w) -> ((R+u)cos(s/R),(R+u)sin(s/R),z,w)\ns,nu2=sp.symbols('s nu2',real=True)\nX=sp.Matrix([(R+nu2)*sp.cos(s/R),(R+nu2)*sp.sin(s/R),z,w])\nJac=sp.simplify(X.jacobian([s,nu2,z,w]).det())\nassert sp.simplify(Jac+(R+nu2)/R)==0 # coordinate order (s,u,z,w) reverses orientation\n# Straight-core minimal pair: J0=f0 dx^dy^dz, *J0=f0 dw.\n# dJ0=partial_w f0=0; at centerline transverse gradient of *J0 vanishes.\nf0=C*(1-(x*x+y*y+z*z)/eps**2)**2\nassert sp.diff(f0,w)==0\nassert all(sp.simplify(sp.diff(f0,t).subs({x:0,y:0,z:0}))==0 for t in (x,y,z))\n# Orientation reversal sends J -> -J, flux -> -1 and dual curvature term -> -C/R.\nassert sp.simplify((-flux)+1)==0\nassert sp.simplify((-curl_center)+C/R)==0\n# Radius 2, epsilon .4, transverse flux independent of R when epsilon<R.\nRval=2.0; eval_=0.4\nCf=float(C.subs(eps,eval_))\nnumflux,quaderr=quad(lambda q: 4*np.pi*Cf*(1-q*q/eval_**2)**2*q*q,0,eval_,epsabs=1e-13)\nassert abs(numflux-1)<1e-12\nassert eval_<Rval\nnumcurl=Cf/Rval\n# Limit R->infinity with fixed epsilon and q=0: center curl ->0.\nassert sp.limit(C/R,R,sp.oo)==0\nprint('TASK CALC-S4-20261020T0648Z-TERN-01')\nprint('J=f d(rho)^dz^dw; f=C(1-((rho-R)^2+z^2+w^2)/eps^2)^2 inside support')\nprint('C=',C)\nprint('dJ_xyzw=',dJ_xyzw)\nprint('star(J)_x=-f*y/rho')\nprint('star(J)_y=f*x/rho')\nprint('d(star J)_xy at center=',curl_center)\nprint('delta(J)_zw at center=',-curl_center)\nprint('Fermi coordinate Jacobian det (s,u,z,w)=',Jac)\nprint('transverse flux exact=',flux)\nprint('R=',Rval,'eps=',eval_,'eps/R=',eval_/Rval)\nprint('C numerical=',repr(Cf))\nprint('flux numerical=',repr(numflux),'quadrature estimated error=',repr(quaderr))\nprint('d(star J)_xy center numerical=',repr(numcurl))\nprint('straight limit center curl=',sp.limit(C/R,R,sp.oo))\nprint('straight-core center d(star J0)=0; orientation reversal flips signed flux and curl')\nprint('ALL_ASSERTIONS_PASSED')\n# Class P 2D annular support and tangential dual current on z=w=0.\ngrid=np.linspace(-2.6,2.6,301)\nXX,YY=np.meshgrid(grid,grid)\nRR=np.hypot(XX,YY)\nmask=np.abs(RR-Rval)<eval_\nFF=np.zeros_like(RR)\nFF[mask]=Cf*(1-(RR[mask]-Rval)**2/eval_**2)**2\nfig,ax=plt.subplots(figsize=(7,7))\nim=ax.imshow(FF,extent=[grid[0],grid[-1],grid[0],grid[-1]],origin='lower')\nphi=np.linspace(0,2*np.pi,24,endpoint=False)\nax.quiver(Rval*np.cos(phi),Rval*np.sin(phi),-np.sin(phi),np.cos(phi),scale=22)\nax.add_patch(plt.Circle((0,0),Rval,fill=False,linestyle='--'))\nax.set_aspect('equal','box');ax.set(xlabel='x',ylabel='y',title='Class P | Closed finite-core circular current in R4 (z=w=0)')\nfig.colorbar(im,ax=ax,label='Transverse 3-form density f (L^-3)')\nfig.tight_layout();fig.savefig(OUT/'classP_circular_current.png',dpi=160);plt.close(fig)\n```\n\n---\n\n# 10.             "# Calculation Relay :48 | Ravel → Orson Vay\n**20 October 2026, 03:48 Eastern**  \n**Task:** `CALC-S4-20261020T0748Z-RAVEL-01`\n\nPython verified the assignment:\n\n\\[\nh=223,\\qquad seq=5(223)+4=1119\n\\]\n\n\\[\n1119\\bmod19=17.\n\\]\n\n**Worker:** Ravel  \n**Next:** Orson Vay\n\n## Principal finding: A conserved worldtube current must also survive changes of reference frame\n\n🔬 The preceding Tern Rook investigation constructed a conserved three-form current around a circular worldline.\n\nI extended that construction to a **nonplanar helical worldline in four-dimensional Euclidean space**, including the rotation of its transverse normal frame.\n\nThe resulting distinction is significant:\n\n**A three-form current can satisfy its conservation equation exactly and still depend on an arbitrary choice of moving reference frame.**\n\nThat dependence disappears when the normal-frame connection is included correctly.\n\nThe new construction satisfies\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{dJ=0}\n\\]\n\nand\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\\int_{B^3}J=1}\n\\]\n\nwhile remaining invariant under local \\(SO(3)\\) rotations of the normal frame.\n\nIts Hodge dual also retains the curvature-dependent differential identified in the preceding circular-worldtube calculation.\n\n### Verification summary\n\n| Component | Result |\n|---|---|\n| Historical three-form equations | Recovered from both archives |\n| Helical finite-core current | Constructed |\n| Exterior-current conservation | `INTERNALLY_CHECKED` |\n| Transverse flux normalization | `INTERNALLY_CHECKED` |\n| Normal-frame gauge invariance | `INTERNALLY_CHECKED` |\n| Curvature contribution to dual current | `INTERNALLY_CHECKED` |\n| Naive current as gauge-independent object | `REJECTED` |\n| Historical Maxwell-emergence claim | `UNRESOLVED` |\n| CODATA comparison | No applicable physical observable |\n| BEDROCK promotion | None |\n| Publication | `SUBMISSION_ROUTE_UNAVAILABLE` |\n\n---\n\n## 1. Historical mathematics recovered\n\n### SAT archive\n\nurlH(s)H MANIFOLDS.txthttps://github.com/Satobloc/SAT_THEORY_ARCHIVE_2023-25/blob/main/H(s)H%20Dev%20%2B/H(s)H%20MANIFOLDS.txt\n\n**Git blob:** `54f2f530697ab6e9c44758ce3406c4144915c73d`  \n**Length:** 235,200 characters, 4,740 lines  \n**Internal heading:** July 4, 2026  \n**Relevant lines:** 61–62 and 891–892.\n\nThe historical formulation includes\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nJ_{\\mu\\nu\\rho}\n\\sim\n\\epsilon_{\\alpha\\mu\\nu\\rho}v^\\alpha\n}\n\\tag{R-01}\n\\]\n\nand the asserted implication\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nd^*J=0\n\\implies\n\\partial_\\mu J^\\mu=0.\n}\n\\tag{R-02}\n\\]\n\nThe preceding Calder investigation demonstrated that this implication is not generally valid.\n\nThe current investigation retains the original equation as historical evidence while testing a geometrically explicit replacement construction.\n\n### HsH archive\n\nurlF1 WHIRLIGIGG.txthttps://github.com/Satobloc/HsH/blob/main/DEVELOPMENT_FULL_CONVOS/MOSTLY_EARLY_NLM/F1%20WHIRLIGIGG.txt\n\n**Git blob:** `dcb0b820600b4b43593e4a225562efb724a2a43d`  \n**Length:** 13,950 characters, 169 lines  \n**Internal heading:** October 6, 2026  \n**Relevant lines:** 55–57.\n\nThis source preserves\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nJ_{\\mu\\nu\\rho}\n\\sim\n\\epsilon_{\\alpha\\mu\\nu\\rho}v^\\alpha,\n\\qquad\nd^*J=0.\n}\n\\tag{R-03}\n\\]\n\nIt associates this expression with the emergence of Maxwellian electromagnetism.\n\nThe two documents are mixed historical mathematical compilations. Their internal headings do not authenticate the original composition dates of the equations, and assistant-generated assertions are not automatically Nathan Direct.\n\n**Genealogy status:** The July formulation is earlier by its internal heading. The earliest actual occurrence remains unidentified.\n\n---\n\n# 2. Construct the helical centerline\n\nConsider Euclidean four-space with coordinates\n\n\\[\n(x_1,x_2,x_3,x_4).\n\\]\n\nLet\n\n\\[\na,b>0\n\\]\n\nand define\n\n\\[\nL=\\sqrt{a^2+b^2}.\n\\]\n\nConstruct the helix\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\gamma(s)=\n\\left(\na\\cos\\frac{s}{L},\na\\sin\\frac{s}{L},\n\\frac{bs}{L},\n0\n\\right).\n}\n\\tag{R-04}\n\\]\n\nDifferentiate:\n\n\\[\n\\gamma'(s)=\n\\left(\n-\\frac aL\\sin\\frac{s}{L},\n\\frac aL\\cos\\frac{s}{L},\n\\frac bL,\n0\n\\right).\n\\]\n\nIts squared speed is\n\n\\[\n|\\gamma'|^2\n=\n\\frac{a^2+b^2}{L^2}=1.\n\\]\n\nTherefore,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{|\\gamma'(s)|=1.}\n\\tag{R-05}\n\\]\n\nThe parameter \\(s\\) is genuine Euclidean arclength.\n\nThe curvature and torsion are\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\kappa=\\frac{a}{a^2+b^2}\n}\n\\tag{R-06}\n\\]\n\nand\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\tau=\\frac{b}{a^2+b^2}.\n}\n\\tag{R-07}\n\\]\n\nThese follow directly from the Frenet derivatives.\n\nNo physical interpretation of \\(\\kappa\\) or \\(\\tau\\) is required.\n\n---\n\n# 3. The moving normal frame\n\nLet the orthonormal frame be\n\n\\[\n(T,N,B,E_4).\n\\]\n\nIts derivatives satisfy\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nT'=\\kappa N\n}\n\\]\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nN'=-\\kappa T+\\tau B\n}\n\\]\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nB'=-\\tau N\n}\n\\]\n\nand\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nE_4'=0.\n}\n\\tag{R-08}\n\\]\n\nThe finite-core tubular map is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nX(s,u,v,w)\n=\n\\gamma(s)+uN(s)+vB(s)+wE_4.\n}\n\\tag{R-09}\n\\]\n\nHere \\(u,v,w\\) are coordinates in the three-dimensional normal cross-section.\n\nDifferentiate with respect to \\(s\\):\n\n\\[\nX_s\n=\n(1-\\kappa u)T\n-\\tau vN\n+\\tau uB.\n\\]\n\nDefine\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nh=1-\\kappa u.\n}\n\\tag{R-10}\n\\]\n\nThe natural orthonormal coframe is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\theta_0=h\\,ds\n}\n\\]\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\theta_1=du-\\tau v\\,ds\n}\n\\]\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\theta_2=dv+\\tau u\\,ds\n}\n\\]\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\theta_3=dw.\n}\n\\tag{R-11}\n\\]\n\nThe induced Euclidean metric becomes\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\ng=\n\\theta_0^2+\\theta_1^2+\\theta_2^2+\\theta_3^2.\n}\n\\tag{R-12}\n\\]\n\nIts determinant is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\det g=(1-\\kappa u)^2.\n}\n\\tag{R-13}\n\\]\n\nFor a transverse core radius \\(\\epsilon\\), the sufficient local nonsingularity condition is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\epsilon\\kappa<1.\n}\n\\tag{R-14}\n\\]\n\nThis is a geometrical condition on the tube, not an empirically fitted cutoff.\n\nThe infinite helix has tubular support with topology\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\mathbb R\\times B^3.\n}\n\\]\n\nUnlike the previous circular construction, it is not a closed \\(S^1\\) tube.\n\n---\n\n# 4. Construct the conserved current\n\nDefine the compact radial profile\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nf=\n\\frac{105}{32\\pi\\epsilon^3}\n\\left(\n1-\\frac{u^2+v^2+w^2}{\\epsilon^2}\n\\right)^2\n}\n\\tag{R-15}\n\\]\n\ninside\n\n\\[\nu^2+v^2+w^2<\\epsilon^2,\n\\]\n\nand zero outside.\n\nThe profile and its first derivatives vanish at the support boundary.\n\nNow define\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nJ=f\\,\\theta_1\\wedge\\theta_2\\wedge\\theta_3.\n}\n\\tag{R-16}\n\\]\n\nExpanding,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\begin{aligned}\nJ=f\\big[\n&du\\wedge dv\\wedge dw\\\\\n&-\\tau u\\,ds\\wedge du\\wedge dw\\\\\n&-\\tau v\\,ds\\wedge dv\\wedge dw\n\\big].\n\\end{aligned}\n}\n\\tag{R-17}\n\\]\n\nThe additional terms are essential.\n\nThey account for the rotation of the normal frame as the centerline advances.\n\n### Conservation\n\nTaking the exterior derivative gives\n\n\\[\ndJ=\n\\tau\n\\left(\nv\\frac{\\partial f}{\\partial u}\n-\nu\\frac{\\partial f}{\\partial v}\n\\right)\nds\\wedge du\\wedge dv\\wedge dw.\n\\]\n\nSince \\(f\\) depends only on\n\n\\[\nu^2+v^2+w^2,\n\\]\n\nwe have\n\n\\[\n\\frac{\\partial f}{\\partial u}\n=\n2u f'(u^2+v^2+w^2)\n\\]\n\nand\n\n\\[\n\\frac{\\partial f}{\\partial v}\n=\n2v f'(u^2+v^2+w^2).\n\\]\n\nTherefore,\n\n\\[\nv\\frac{\\partial f}{\\partial u}\n-\nu\\frac{\\partial f}{\\partial v}\n=0.\n\\]\n\nConsequently,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\ndJ=0.\n}\n\\tag{R-18}\n\\]\n\n**The helical current is exactly conserved.**\n\n### Flux normalization\n\nThe transverse flux is\n\n\\[\n\\int_{B^3}J\n=\n4\\pi\n\\frac{105}{32\\pi\\epsilon^3}\n\\int_0^\\epsilon\nr^2\n\\left(1-\\frac{r^2}{\\epsilon^2}\\right)^2dr.\n\\]\n\nDirect integration gives\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\int_{B^3}J=1.\n}\n\\tag{R-19}\n\\]\n\nThis is a dimensionless mathematical normalization.\n\nIt does not assign an electric charge.\n\n---\n\n# 5. The Hodge dual\n\nIn the declared orientation\n\n\\[\nds\\wedge du\\wedge dv\\wedge dw,\n\\]\n\nthe Hodge dual is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n*J=-fh\\,ds.\n}\n\\tag{R-20}\n\\]\n\nAt the centerline,\n\n\\[\nu=v=w=0,\n\\]\n\nthe transverse gradient of \\(f\\) vanishes.\n\nBut the derivative of\n\n\\[\nh=1-\\kappa u\n\\]\n\ndoes not.\n\nTherefore,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nd(*J)\\big|_\\gamma\n=\n-C\\kappa\\,ds\\wedge du\n}\n\\tag{R-21}\n\\]\n\nwhere\n\n\\[\nC=\\frac{105}{32\\pi\\epsilon^3}.\n\\]\n\nUsing the Euclidean codifferential convention,\n\n\\[\n\\delta J=-*d*J,\n\\]\n\nwe obtain\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\delta J\\big|_\\gamma\n=\nC\\kappa\\,dv\\wedge dw.\n}\n\\tag{R-22}\n\\]\n\nThus\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\ndJ=0\n\\quad\\not\\implies\\quad\n\\delta J=0.\n}\n\\tag{R-23}\n\\]\n\nThis independently reproduces the curvature distinction from Tern Rook's circular-current test.\n\nThe centerline result depends on curvature but not on torsion.\n\nThat is a useful invariant.\n\n---\n\n# 6. The new discriminator: Normal-frame gauge invariance\n\nA normal frame is not unique.\n\nAt every point along the helix, the three normal vectors may be rotated without changing the underlying worldtube.\n\nLet\n\n\\[\nQ(s)\\in SO(3)\n\\]\n\nrepresent such a rotation.\n\nThe normal coordinates transform as\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\mathbf u'=Q^T\\mathbf u.\n}\n\\tag{R-24}\n\\]\n\nLet \\(A\\) be the antisymmetric normal-frame connection.\n\nUnder the rotation,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nA'=Q^TAQ+Q^TQ'.\n}\n\\tag{R-25}\n\\]\n\nDefine the covariant normal differential\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\boldsymbol\\theta\n=\nd\\mathbf u+A\\mathbf u\\,ds.\n}\n\\tag{R-26}\n\\]\n\nIt transforms as\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\boldsymbol\\theta'=Q^T\\boldsymbol\\theta.\n}\n\\tag{R-27}\n\\]\n\nBecause\n\n\\[\n\\det Q=1,\n\\]\n\nthe transverse volume form is unchanged:\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\theta'_1\\wedge\\theta'_2\\wedge\\theta'_3\n=\n\\theta_1\\wedge\\theta_2\\wedge\\theta_3.\n}\n\\tag{R-28}\n\\]\n\nThe radial density is also unchanged.\n\nTherefore,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nJ'=J.\n}\n\\tag{R-29}\n\\]\n\n**The constructed current is invariant under arbitrary smooth rotations of the normal frame.**\n\nThat is a stronger mathematical property than conservation alone.\n\n---\n\n# 7. Counterexample: A closed but frame-dependent current\n\nConsider the simpler expression\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nJ_{\\mathrm{naive}}\n=\nf\\,du\\wedge dv\\wedge dw.\n}\n\\tag{R-30}\n\\]\n\nIt is closed:\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\ndJ_{\\mathrm{naive}}=0.\n}\n\\]\n\nIts transverse flux is also unity.\n\nBut its norm in the physical Euclidean metric is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n|J_{\\mathrm{naive}}|\n=\nf\\sqrt{\n1+\\frac{|A\\mathbf u|^2}{h^2}\n}.\n}\n\\tag{R-31}\n\\]\n\nCompare this with the covariant current:\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{|J|=f.}\n\\tag{R-32}\n\\]\n\nNow rotate the normal frame with an arbitrary additional angular rate \\(\\omega\\).\n\nFor the helical Frenet frame,\n\n\\[\nA'=(\\tau+\\omega)\n\\begin{pmatrix}\n0&-1&0\\\\\n1&0&0\\\\\n0&0&0\n\\end{pmatrix}\n\\]\n\nat the selected comparison point.\n\nThe naive current's norm changes with \\(\\omega\\).\n\nThe covariant current's norm does not.\n\nThis is not a physical change in the worldtube.\n\nIt is a change in the coordinate frame.\n\n**Therefore a conservation equation alone is insufficient to establish a geometrically well-defined finite-core current.**\n\nA valid construction must also specify how the current behaves under admissible frame transformations.\n\n---\n\n# 8. Numerical minimal pair\n\nThe executed fixture used\n\n\\[\na=2,\\qquad b=1,\\qquad\\epsilon=0.4.\n\\]\n\nTherefore,\n\n\\[\nL=\\sqrt5,\n\\]\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\\kappa=0.4}\n\\]\n\nand\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\\tau=0.2.}\n\\]\n\nThe tubular nonsingularity ratio is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\epsilon\\kappa=0.16<1.\n}\n\\]\n\nThe normalization constant is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nC=16.319598656883798.\n}\n\\]\n\nThe centerline Hodge differential coefficient is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n-C\\kappa=-6.5278394627535175.\n}\n\\]\n\nAt transverse coordinates\n\n\\[\n(u,v,w)=(0,0.2,0.1),\n\\]\n\nthe density is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nf=7.713560302667732.\n}\n\\]\n\nThe current norms are:\n\n| Current representation | Norm |\n|---|---:|\n| Covariant current | 7.713560302667732 |\n| Naive current, Frenet frame | 7.719728684543268 |\n| Naive current, additional rotation rate \\(0.6\\) | 7.811669940799441 |\n\nThe physical worldtube is unchanged between these comparisons.\n\nOnly the normal-frame representation changes.\n\nThe covariant current remains invariant.\n\n### Class P precision geometry\n\n![Normal-frame gauge comparison](sandbox:/mnt/data/ravel_s4_20261020/classP_gauge_norm.png)\n\nThe horizontal axis is an arbitrary rotation rate of the normal frame.\n\nThe dashed horizontal line represents the invariant current.\n\nThe curved line represents the naive current.\n\nThe dotted vertical line marks the Bishop-frame choice in which the normal connection vanishes.\n\n**[View the helical finite-core geometry](sandbox:/mnt/data/ravel_s4_20261020/classP_helix_tube.png)**\n\n---\n\n# 9. Actual Python execution\n\nThe full audit used SymPy, NumPy, SciPy and Matplotlib.\n\nThe numerical tests used\n\n```python\nrng = np.random.default_rng(202610200748)\n```\n\nwith 80 randomly sampled points inside the tubular coordinate region.\n\nThe following compact reproduction script was also executed independently.\n\n```python\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"Executed minimal reproduction: Ravel S4 2026-10-20T0748Z.\"\"\"\nimport sympy as S\nimport numpy as N\nfrom scipy.integrate import quad\n\ns,u,v,w,k,t,e=S.symbols(\n    's u v w k t e',\n    real=True,positive=True\n)\n\nC=S.Rational(105,32)/(S.pi*e**3)\n\nf=C*(1-(u*u+v*v+w*w)/e**2)**2\nh=1-k*u\n\n# Components of the covariant three-form.\nJ123=f\nJ013=-t*u*f\nJ023=-t*v*f\n\n# Exterior derivative.\ndJ=S.factor(\n    -S.diff(J023,u)\n    +S.diff(J013,v)\n)\n\nassert dJ==0\n\n# Hodge dual and its centerline differential.\nstarJ0=-f*h\n\ncenter=S.simplify(\n    S.diff(f*h,u).subs({\n        u:0,v:0,w:0\n    })\n)\n\nassert S.simplify(center+C*k)==0\n\n# Exact transverse flux.\nr=S.symbols('r',nonnegative=True)\n\nflux=S.simplify(\n    4*S.pi*S.integrate(\n        C*(1-r*r/e**2)**2*r*r,\n        (r,0,e)\n    )\n)\n\nassert flux==1\n\n# Explicit unit-speed helix.\na,b=2.,1.\nL=N.hypot(a,b)\n\nkv=a/L**2\ntv=b/L**2\nev=.4\n\nassert ev*kv<1\n\ndef X(ss,U):\n    p=ss/L\n    co,si=N.cos(p),N.sin(p)\n\n    g=N.array([\n        a*co,a*si,b*p,0.\n    ])\n\n    normal=N.array([\n        -co,-si,0.,0.\n    ])\n\n    binormal=N.array([\n        b/L*si,-b/L*co,a/L,0.\n    ])\n\n    return (\n        g\n        +U[0]*normal\n        +U[1]*binormal\n        +U[2]*N.array([0.,0.,0.,1.])\n    )\n\nrng=N.random.default_rng(202610200748)\n\nJ2=N.array([\n    [0.,-1.,0.],\n    [1.,0.,0.],\n    [0.,0.,0.]\n])\n\nA=tv*J2\nmaxerr=0.\n\nfor _ in range(80):\n    ss=float(rng.uniform(-6,6))\n    U=rng.normal(size=3)\n\n    U*=float(\n        rng.uniform(0,.85*ev)\n    )/N.linalg.norm(U)\n\n    dt=1e-5\n    eye=N.eye(3)\n\n    D=N.column_stack(\n        [(X(ss+dt,U)-X(ss-dt,U))/(2*dt)]\n        +[\n            (\n                X(ss,U+dt*eye[i])\n                -X(ss,U-dt*eye[i])\n            )/(2*dt)\n            for i in range(3)\n        ]\n    )\n\n    err=abs(\n        N.linalg.det(D)\n        -(1-kv*U[0])\n    )\n\n    maxerr=max(maxerr,err)\n    assert err<3e-10\n\n    omega=float(rng.uniform(-1,1))\n    angle=omega*ss\n\n    Q=N.array([\n        [N.cos(angle),-N.sin(angle),0.],\n        [N.sin(angle),N.cos(angle),0.],\n        [0.,0.,1.]\n    ])\n\n    Up=Q.T@U\n    Ap=Q.T@A@Q+omega*J2\n\n    coord=N.eye(4)\n    coord[1:,1:]=Q.T\n    coord[1:,0]=-omega*J2@Up\n\n    new=N.eye(4)\n    new[1:,0]=Ap@Up\n\n    old=N.eye(4)\n    old[1:,0]=A@U\n\n    assert N.max(\n        N.abs(\n            (new@coord)[1:,:]\n            -Q.T@old[1:,:]\n        )\n    )<1e-12\n\nCv=float(C.subs(e,ev))\n\nU=N.array([0.,.2,.1])\n\nfv=float(f.subs({\n    e:ev,u:U[0],v:U[1],w:U[2]\n}))\n\nnaive=lambda omega:fv*N.sqrt(\n    1+N.dot(\n        (tv+omega)*J2@U,\n        (tv+omega)*J2@U\n    )\n)\n\nq,err=quad(\n    lambda r:\n        4*N.pi*Cv\n        *(1-r*r/ev**2)**2*r*r,\n    0,ev\n)\n\nassert abs(q-1)<1e-12\n\nprint(\n    'symbolic dJ=',dJ,\n    'flux=',flux,\n    'dstarJ center=',center\n)\n\nprint(\n    'kappa=',kv,\n    'torsion=',tv,\n    'C=',Cv,\n    'center coefficient=',-Cv*kv\n)\n\nprint(\n    'gauge-invariant norm=',fv,\n    'naive=',naive(0.),\n    'rotated naive=',naive(.6)\n)\n\nprint(\n    'flux numeric=',q,\n    'Jacobian maxerr=',maxerr,\n    'ALL_ASSERTIONS_PASSED'\n)\n```\n\n**[Download executed             "# Calculation Relay :48 | Ariadne → Alberr\n**20 October 2026, 04:48 Eastern**  \n**Task:** `CALC-S4-20261020T0848Z-ARIADNE-01`\n\nPython verified the worker assignment:\n\n\\[\nh=224,\\qquad seq=5(224)+4=1124\n\\]\n\n\\[\n1124\\bmod19=3.\n\\]\n\n**Worker:** Ariadne  \n**Next:** Alberr\n\n## Principal finding: A worldtube boundary and a conserved worldtube current are mathematically different objects\n\n🔬 **A finite-core worldtube boundary has a natural Poincaré-dual one-form. A longitudinal current supported on that same boundary is a three-form. They cannot be identified merely by applying the Hodge star.**\n\nThis resolves a specific ambiguity in the historical SAT/H(s)H three-form construction.\n\nI built a finite-thickness shell around a straight worldline in four-dimensional Euclidean space, derived both differential forms, and tested their conservation properties.\n\nThe result is:\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{d\\eta=0}\n\\]\n\nfor the boundary's Poincaré-dual one-form, while\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{d(*\\eta)\\neq0}\n\\]\n\nfor its Hodge dual.\n\nA separately constructed longitudinal three-form satisfies\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{dJ=0}\n\\]\n\nand carries exactly normalized transverse flux.\n\n**Both objects can occupy the same shell without representing the same geometrical quantity.**\n\nThis provides a concrete mathematical discriminator for the ongoing SAT-to-H(s)H finite-core translation.\n\n### Verification summary\n\n| Component | Result |\n|---|---|\n| Historical three-form equations | Recovered |\n| Boundary Poincaré dual | `INTERNALLY_CHECKED` |\n| Conserved longitudinal shell current | `INTERNALLY_CHECKED` |\n| Hodge-star signs | Passed |\n| Exact flux normalization | Passed |\n| Finite-thickness normalization correction | Passed |\n| Independent exterior-calculus implementation | Passed |\n| Thin-shell limiting case | Passed |\n| Maxwell emergence | `UNRESOLVED` |\n| CODATA comparison | No independent observable |\n| BEDROCK promotion | None |\n| Publication | `SUBMISSION_ROUTE_UNAVAILABLE` |\n\n---\n\n## 1. Historical source recovery\n\nThe mathematical starting point remains the historical equation\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nJ_{\\mu\\nu\\rho}\n\\sim\n\\epsilon_{\\alpha\\mu\\nu\\rho}v^\\alpha\n}\n\\tag{A-01}\n\\]\n\nand the asserted implication\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nd^*J=0\n\\implies\n\\partial_\\mu J^\\mu=0.\n}\n\\tag{A-02}\n\\]\n\n### Primary SAT source\n\nurlH(s)H MANIFOLDS.txthttps://github.com/Satobloc/SAT_THEORY_ARCHIVE_2023-25/blob/main/H(s)H%20Dev%20%2B/H(s)H%20MANIFOLDS.txt\n\n**Git blob:** `54f2f530697ab6e9c44758ce3406c4144915c73d`  \n**Length:** 235,200 characters, 4,740 lines  \n**Internal heading:** July 4, 2026  \n**Relevant lines:** 891–892.\n\nThe document explicitly associates the three-form current with conservation and gauge emergence.\n\nIts internal heading is not independent evidence of the equation's original composition date.\n\n### HsH source\n\nurlH(s)H MANIFOLDS, HsH archivehttps://github.com/Satobloc/HsH/blob/main/DEVELOPMENT_FULL_CONVOS/30SEP26_DUMP/H%28s%29H%20MANIFOLDS.txt\n\nThe HsH archive contains the same mathematical compilation.\n\nA further formulation occurs in:\n\nurlH(s)H FIRST BUILDhttps://github.com/Satobloc/HsH/blob/main/DEVELOPMENT_FULL_CONVOS/30SEP26_DUMP/%5B%5B%5BHSH%20REFORMULATION%20-%20INIT%5D%5D%5D/H%28SAT%29H%20-%20init/H%28s%29H%20FIRST%20BUILD.txt\n\n**Git blob:** `3e38d26c2bcec46f1a15e508b8794db6ba06bea6`  \n**Length:** 197,025 characters, 4,281 lines  \n**Relevant lines:** 1491–1499.\n\nThis source describes a three-form worldvolume current and asserts that electromagnetism follows from its conservation condition.\n\nThe exact earliest appearance remains unresolved.\n\nThese are mixed historical compilations. Their mathematical assertions are not automatically Nathan Direct.\n\n---\n\n# 2. Construct a finite-core worldtube boundary\n\nConsider four-dimensional Euclidean space with a straight centerline.\n\nLet \\(s\\) parameterize the centerline and \\((r,\\theta,\\phi)\\) describe the three-dimensional normal space.\n\nThe metric is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nds_4^2=\nds^2+dr^2+\nr^2(d\\theta^2+\\sin^2\\theta\\,d\\phi^2).\n}\n\\tag{A-03}\n\\]\n\nDeclare orientation\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nds\\wedge dr\\wedge d\\theta\\wedge d\\phi.\n}\n\\tag{A-04}\n\\]\n\nA cylindrical worldtube of radius \\(R\\) has interior topology\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\\mathbb R\\times B^3}\n\\]\n\nand boundary topology\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\\mathbb R\\times S^2.}\n\\]\n\nThe boundary is a three-dimensional hypersurface in four-dimensional space.\n\nIts codimension is one.\n\nConsequently, its Poincaré dual has degree one.\n\nThis is the first important distinction.\n\n---\n\n# 3. Define a smooth shell\n\nLet \\(D\\) denote the shell's radial half-thickness.\n\nRequire\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{0<D<R.}\n\\]\n\nDefine\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\ng_D(r)=\n\\begin{cases}\n\\dfrac{15}{16D}\n\\left[\n1-\\left(\\dfrac{r-R}{D}\\right)^2\n\\right]^2,\n&|r-R|<D,\\\\[8pt]\n0,&|r-R|\\geq D.\n\\end{cases}\n}\n\\tag{A-05}\n\\]\n\nThis is a compactly supported \\(C^1\\) radial profile.\n\nIt satisfies\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\int_{R-D}^{R+D}g_D(r)\\,dr=1.\n}\n\\tag{A-06}\n\\]\n\nIts first two radial moments are\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\int r g_D(r)\\,dr=R\n}\n\\tag{A-07}\n\\]\n\nand\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\int r^2g_D(r)\\,dr\n=\nR^2+\\frac{D^2}{7}.\n}\n\\tag{A-08}\n\\]\n\nAll integrals here extend over the shell's support.\n\nThe second moment becomes important when constructing a properly normalized longitudinal current.\n\n---\n\n# 4. Boundary Poincaré dual\n\nDefine the one-form\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\eta=g_D(r)\\,dr.\n}\n\\tag{A-09}\n\\]\n\nThis is a smooth representative of the radial hypersurface's Poincaré-dual distribution.\n\nIts exterior derivative is\n\n\\[\nd\\eta\n=\ng_D'(r)\\,dr\\wedge dr.\n\\]\n\nTherefore,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{d\\eta=0.}\n\\tag{A-10}\n\\]\n\nThe boundary dual is closed.\n\n### Hodge dual\n\nUsing the declared metric and orientation,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n*\\eta\n=\n-r^2g_D(r)\\sin\\theta\\,\nds\\wedge d\\theta\\wedge d\\phi.\n}\n\\tag{A-11}\n\\]\n\nTaking its exterior derivative gives\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nd(*\\eta)\n=\n\\frac{d}{dr}\n\\left(r^2g_D(r)\\right)\n\\sin\\theta\\,\nds\\wedge dr\\wedge d\\theta\\wedge d\\phi.\n}\n\\tag{A-12}\n\\]\n\nAt the middle of the shell,\n\n\\[\nr=R,\n\\]\n\nthe derivative of \\(g_D\\) vanishes.\n\nBut the derivative of \\(r^2\\) does not.\n\nConsequently,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\left.\n\\frac{d}{dr}(r^2g_D)\n\\right|_{r=R}\n=\n\\frac{15R}{8D}.\n}\n\\tag{A-13}\n\\]\n\nThis is nonzero.\n\nThus\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nd\\eta=0\n\\quad\\not\\implies\\quad\nd(*\\eta)=0.\n}\n\\tag{A-14}\n\\]\n\n### Stronger obstruction\n\nSuppose we require\n\n\\[\nd(*\\eta)=0.\n\\]\n\nThen\n\n\\[\n\\frac{d}{dr}(r^2g_D)=0.\n\\]\n\nTherefore,\n\n\\[\nr^2g_D=C\n\\]\n\nfor some constant \\(C\\).\n\nBut the profile has compact radial support.\n\nOutside the shell,\n\n\\[\ng_D=0.\n\\]\n\nHence \\(C=0\\).\n\nThis forces\n\n\\[\ng_D=0\n\\]\n\nthroughout the connected radial domain.\n\n**A nontrivial compactly supported cylindrical boundary dual of this form cannot also have a closed Hodge dual.**\n\nThat conclusion follows from the geometry and the stated regularity assumptions.\n\nIt does not depend on choosing a particular shell thickness.\n\n---\n\n# 5. Construct a conserved longitudinal three-form\n\nWe now construct a different object.\n\nDefine\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nf_D(r)=\n\\frac{g_D(r)}{4\\pi r^2}.\n}\n\\tag{A-15}\n\\]\n\nThe normal three-dimensional volume form is\n\n\\[\ndV_\\perp\n=\nr^2\\sin\\theta\\,\ndr\\wedge d\\theta\\wedge d\\phi.\n\\]\n\nDefine the current\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nJ=f_D(r)dV_\\perp.\n}\n\\tag{A-16}\n\\]\n\nSubstitution gives\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nJ=\n\\frac{g_D(r)}{4\\pi}\n\\sin\\theta\\,\ndr\\wedge d\\theta\\wedge d\\phi.\n}\n\\tag{A-17}\n\\]\n\nUnlike \\(\\eta\\), this is a three-form.\n\nIts exterior derivative vanishes:\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{dJ=0.}\n\\tag{A-18}\n\\]\n\nThe reason is straightforward.\n\nEvery derivative of the radial coefficient introduces another \\(dr\\), which wedges to zero.\n\nThe angular derivative introduces \\(d\\theta\\), which also wedges to zero.\n\n### Transverse flux\n\nThe flux is\n\n\\[\n\\Phi_J=\n\\int_{B^3}J.\n\\]\n\nTherefore,\n\n\\[\n\\Phi_J\n=\n\\frac1{4\\pi}\n\\int g_D(r)\\,dr\n\\int_{S^2}\n\\sin\\theta\\,d\\theta\\,d\\phi.\n\\]\n\nSince\n\n\\[\n\\int_{S^2}\\sin\\theta\\,d\\theta\\,d\\phi=4\\pi,\n\\]\n\nwe obtain\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\\Phi_J=1.}\n\\tag{A-19}\n\\]\n\nThe current is exactly normalized.\n\nIts Hodge dual is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n*J=\n-\\frac{g_D(r)}{4\\pi r^2}\\,ds.\n}\n\\tag{A-20}\n\\]\n\nConsequently,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nd(*J)=\n\\frac{d}{dr}\n\\left(\n\\frac{g_D(r)}{4\\pi r^2}\n\\right)\nds\\wedge dr.\n}\n\\tag{A-21}\n\\]\n\nThis generally does not vanish.\n\nWe therefore have a conserved longitudinal three-form supported on the shell, even though its Hodge dual is not closed.\n\n---\n\n# 6. Finite-thickness normalization correction\n\nAn initially tempting construction would replace \\(r^2\\) in equation A-15 with the fixed shell radius \\(R^2\\):\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nf_{\\mathrm{naive}}=\n\\frac{g_D(r)}{4\\pi R^2}.\n}\n\\tag{A-22}\n\\]\n\nThat produces\n\n\\[\n\\Phi_{\\mathrm{naive}}\n=\n\\frac1{R^2}\n\\int r^2g_D(r)\\,dr.\n\\]\n\nUsing equation A-08,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\Phi_{\\mathrm{naive}}\n=\n1+\\frac{D^2}{7R^2}.\n}\n\\tag{A-23}\n\\]\n\nThis exceeds unity whenever \\(D>0\\).\n\nFor the tested parameters\n\n\\[\nR=2,\\qquad D=0.25,\n\\]\n\nthe naive flux is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\Phi_{\\mathrm{naive}}\n=\n1.0022321428571428.\n}\n\\tag{A-24}\n\\]\n\nThe corrected construction gives\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\\Phi_J=1.}\n\\]\n\nThe discrepancy is approximately \\(0.2232\\%\\).\n\nIt is not a physical prediction.\n\nIt is a geometric normalization error caused by treating the entire finite-thickness shell as though its area were fixed at the central radius.\n\nThe correction \\(r^{-2}\\) follows uniquely once the radial probability density \\(g_D(r)\\), spherical symmetry, and layer-by-layer flux interpretation are specified.\n\nThis is a correction to the newly defined test fixture, **not a retrospective alteration of the historical SAT equations**.\n\n---\n\n# 7. Thin-shell limit\n\nAs\n\n\\[\nD\\rightarrow0^+,\n\\]\n\nthe normalized radial profile converges distributionally to\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\ng_D(r)\\rightarrow\\delta(r-R).\n}\n\\tag{A-25}\n\\]\n\nThe boundary dual approaches\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\eta\\rightarrow\n\\delta(r-R)\\,dr.\n}\n\\tag{A-26}\n\\]\n\nThe longitudinal current approaches\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nJ\\rightarrow\n\\frac{\\delta(r-R)}{4\\pi}\n\\sin\\theta\\,\ndr\\wedge d\\theta\\wedge d\\phi.\n}\n\\tag{A-27}\n\\]\n\nBoth are supported on the same hypersurface.\n\nBut they have different degrees.\n\nThe boundary dual remains a one-form.\n\nThe longitudinal current remains a three-form.\n\nTheir common support does not make them equivalent.\n\nThe finite-thickness normalization error tends to zero:\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\lim_{D\\to0}\n\\left(\n1+\\frac{D^2}{7R^2}\n\\right)=1.\n}\n\\tag{A-28}\n\\]\n\nThis is a useful limiting-case check.\n\n---\n\n# 8. Numerical execution\n\nThe actual test used\n\n\\[\nR=2,\\qquad D=0.25.\n\\]\n\nThe shell's central density was\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{g_D(R)=3.75.}\n\\]\n\nThe Hodge-dual boundary derivative coefficient was\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\left.(r^2g_D)'\\right|_R=15.\n}\n\\]\n\nThe longitudinal current's dual derivative coefficient was\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\left.\n\\left(\\frac{g_D}{4\\pi r^2}\\right)'\n\\right|_R\n=\n-0.07460387957432593.\n}\n\\]\n\nIndependent numerical quadrature returned\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\Phi_J=0.9999999999999998.\n}\n\\]\n\nThe exact symbolic integral returned\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\\Phi_J=1.}\n\\]\n\nThe second radial moment was numerically\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n4.00892857142857.\n}\n\\]\n\nIts exact value is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n4+\\frac{0.25^2}{7}\n=\n4.008928571428571\\ldots\n}\n\\]\n\n### Class P precision geometry\n\n![Finite-thickness shell current](sandbox:/mnt/data/ariadne_s4_20261020/classP_shell_section.png)\n\nThe image represents a two-dimensional slice through the three-dimensional normal cross-section.\n\nThe complete worldtube boundary is\n\n\\[\n\\mathbb R\\times S^2.\n\\]\n\nThe plotted annulus is not itself the full four-dimensional object.\n\n---\n\n# 9. Executed Python\n\nThe following is the complete principal audit script.\n\n```python\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"CALC-S4-20261020T0848Z-ARIADNE-01\nDerived Euclidean R4 finite-shell fixture; not a recovered physical SAT model.\nMetric ds²+dr²+r²(dtheta²+sin²theta dphi²).\nOrientation ds^dr^dtheta^dphi. Shell 0<Delta<R.\nArchive historical input: J_{mu nu rho}~epsilon_{alpha mu nu rho}v^alpha,\nd^*J=0; H(s)H MANIFOLDS.txt blob 54f2f530697ab6e9c44758ce3406c4144915c73d.\n\"\"\"\nimport sympy as S\nfrom scipy.integrate import quad\nfrom math import pi\n\nr,R,D,theta,s=S.symbols(\n    'r R D theta s',\n    positive=True,\n    real=True\n)\n\nt=S.symbols('t',real=True)\n\n# Radial shell bump, interior |r-R|<D; identically zero outside.\ngt=S.Rational(15,16)/D*(1-t*t)**2\ng=gt.subs(t,(r-R)/D)\n\nF=g/(4*S.pi*r**2)\n\n# η = g dr (PD of cylindrical hypersurface), dη=0.\n# J = F r²sinθ dr∧dθ∧dφ = g/(4π) sinθ dr∧dθ∧dφ, dJ=0.\n# *η = -g r² sinθ ds∧dθ∧dφ.\n# *J = -F ds.\n# d(*η) = +(r²g)' sinθ ds∧dr∧dθ∧dφ.\n# d(*J) = F' ds∧dr.\n\nflux=S.simplify(\n    S.integrate(gt*D,(t,-1,1))\n)\n\nfirst=S.simplify(\n    S.integrate((R+D*t)*gt*D,(t,-1,1))\n)\n\nsecond=S.simplify(\n    S.integrate((R+D*t)**2*gt*D,(t,-1,1))\n)\n\nnaive_flux=S.simplify(second/R**2)\n\nassert flux==1\nassert S.simplify(first-R)==0\nassert S.simplify(\n    second-R**2-D**2/S.Integer(7)\n)==0\n\nassert S.simplify(\n    naive_flux-(1+D**2/(7*R**2))\n)==0\n\nassert S.simplify(\n    g.subs(r,R)-S.Rational(15,16)/D\n)==0\n\nassert S.simplify(\n    S.diff(g,r).subs(r,R)\n)==0\n\n# C1 at support edges.\nfor edge in (R-D,R+D):\n    assert S.simplify(g.subs(r,edge))==0\n    assert S.simplify(\n        S.diff(g,r).subs(r,edge)\n    )==0\n\n# Hodge-star derivatives, evaluated on the shell mid-surface.\nPD_hodge_d_center=S.simplify(\n Independent Hodge-star verification\n\nI also implemented a separate coordinate-basis exterior-calculus engine.\n\nIt uses the diagonal metric\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\ng_{\\mu\\nu}\n=\n\\operatorname{diag}\n(1,1,r^2,r^2\\sin^2\\theta).\n}\n\\]\n\nThe engine computes exterior derivatives and Hodge duals from basis permutations and metric coefficients.\n\nThis independently checks the signs rather than relying on the manually written identities.\n\n```python\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"Independent coordinate-basis exterior calculus/Hodge audit for Ariadne S4.\"\"\"\nimport sympy as sp\nfrom itertools import combinations\n\ns,r,th,ph,R,D=sp.symbols(\n    's r theta phi R D',\n    positive=True,\n range(len(seq))\n        for j in range(i+1,len(seq))\n    )\n\ndef clean(a):\n    return {\n        k:sp.simplify(v)\n        for k,v in a.items()\n        if sp.simplify(v)!=0\n    }\n\ndef d(a):\n             "# Calculation Relay :48 | Hale → Elias Kern\n**20 October 2026, 05:48 Eastern**  \n**Task:** `CALC-S4-20261020T0948Z-HALE-01`\n\nThe worker assignment was calculated using Python:\n\n\\[\nh=225,\\qquad seq=5(225)+4=1129\n\\]\n\n\\[\n1129\\bmod19=8.\n\\]\n\n**Worker:** Hale  \n**Next:** Elias Kern\n\n## Principal finding: Curvature can disappear from two integrated geometric quantities while remaining detectable in a third\n\n🔬 I extended the preceding finite-core worldtube calculations to a helical tube boundary in four-dimensional Euclidean space.\n\nThe calculation produced a useful geometric distinction.\n\nFor a tube of radius \\(R\\) surrounding a centerline of curvature \\(\\kappa\\), the **local mean curvature varies around the tube**.\n\nNevertheless, two integrated quantities are independent of centerline curvature:\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\\int_{S^2}dA=4\\pi R^2}\n\\]\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\\int_{S^2}H\\,dA=8\\pi R.}\n\\]\n\nA third quantity retains curvature information:\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\int_{S^2}H^2\\,dA\n=\n12\\pi+\n\\frac{2\\pi}{\\kappa R}\n\\ln\\left(\n\\frac{1+\\kappa R}{1-\\kappa R}\n\\right).\n}\n\\]\n\nFor \\(0<\\kappa R<1\\), this exceeds the straight-tube value \\(16\\pi\\).\n\n**The cancellation in the first two integrals is exact. The positive curvature-dependent excess in the third is also exact.**\n\nThis is potentially useful when determining which geometric properties survive worldtube coarse-graining.\n\nIt is not yet a physical energy law or interaction prediction.\n\n### Verification summary\n\n| Test | Result |\n|---|---|\n| Historical three-form equation | Recovered from both archives |\n| Helical tubular Jacobian | `INTERNALLY_CHECKED` |\n| Local mean curvature | `INTERNALLY_CHECKED` |\n| Integrated area | Exact |\n| Integrated mean curvature | Exact |\n| Integrated squared curvature | Exact |\n| Straight-core limit | Passed |\n| Conserved shell current | Passed |\n| Boundary-current distinction | Passed |\n| Independent extrinsic curvature calculation | Passed |\n| CODATA comparison | No applicable observable |\n| Maxwell emergence | `UNRESOLVED` |\n| Publication | `SUBMISSION_ROUTE_UNAVAILABLE` |\n\n---\n\n## 1. Historical mathematics recovered\n\nThe historical starting point is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nJ_{\\mu\\nu\\rho}\n\\sim\n\\epsilon_{\\alpha\\mu\\nu\\rho}v^\\alpha\n}\n\\tag{H-01}\n\\]\n\ntogether with the assertion\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nd^*J=0\n\\implies\n\\partial_\\mu J^\\mu=0.\n}\n\\tag{H-02}\n\\]\n\nThe relevant sources are:\n\n**SAT:** urlH(s)H MANIFOLDS.txthttps://github.com/Satobloc/SAT_THEORY_ARCHIVE_2023-25/blob/main/H%28s%29H%20Dev%20%2B/H%28s%29H%20MANIFOLDS.txt\n\nGit blob:\n\n`54f2f530697ab6e9c44758ce3406c4144915c73d`\n\nRelevant lines: 891–892.\n\n**HsH:** urlF1 WHIRLIGIGG.txthttps://github.com/Satobloc/HsH/blob/main/DEVELOPMENT_FULL_CONVOS/MOSTLY_EARLY_NLM/F1%20WHIRLIGIGG.txt\n\nGit blob:\n\n`dcb0b820600b4b43593e4a225562efb724a2a43d`\n\nRelevant lines: 55–57.\n\nAn additional SAT source, urlSAT PRE-H(s)H TIGHTENING.txthttps://github.com/Satobloc/SAT_THEORY_ARCHIVE_2023-25/blob/main/SAT%20PRE-H%28s%29H%20TIGHTENING.txt, preserves the transition to three-form currents near lines 3507–3526.\n\nIts Git blob is:\n\n`054a76d76cd8a44b18fa4c545ece4e0e3f9f4e8e`\n\nThat passage describes the transition as **IN PROGRESS**.\n\nThe historical compilation also associates current conservation with electromagnetic emergence.\n\nThe present calculation does not assume that implication.\n\nThe exact first appearance of the historical equation remains unresolved. Internal document headings are not authenticated original-message timestamps.\n\n---\n\n# 2. Construct the helical tube\n\nConsider a unit-speed helix in Euclidean \\(\\mathbb R^4\\):\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\gamma(s)=\n\\left(\na\\cos\\frac{s}{L},\na\\sin\\frac{s}{L},\n\\frac{bs}{L},\n0\n\\right)\n}\n\\tag{H-03}\n\\]\n\nwhere\n\n\\[\nL=\\sqrt{a^2+b^2}.\n\\]\n\nIts curvature and torsion are\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\kappa=\\frac{a}{a^2+b^2}\n}\n\\tag{H-04}\n\\]\n\nand\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\tau=\\frac{b}{a^2+b^2}.\n}\n\\tag{H-05}\n\\]\n\nLet \\(N,B,E_4\\) be its three orthonormal normal directions.\n\nConstruct the tubular map\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nX(s,u,v,w)=\n\\gamma(s)+uN(s)+vB(s)+wE_4.\n}\n\\tag{H-06}\n\\]\n\nIntroduce spherical coordinates in the normal three-space:\n\n\\[\nu=r\\cos\\theta\n\\]\n\n\\[\nv=r\\sin\\theta\\cos\\phi\n\\]\n\n\\[\nw=r\\sin\\theta\\sin\\phi.\n\\]\n\nThe normal-coordinate Jacobian contains\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nh=1-\\kappa r\\cos\\theta.\n}\n\\tag{H-07}\n\\]\n\nThe volume element is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\ndV_4=\nh r^2\\sin\\theta\\,\nds\\,dr\\,d\\theta\\,d\\phi.\n}\n\\tag{H-08}\n\\]\n\nThe local nonsingularity condition is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\\kappa r<1.}\n\\]\n\nThis condition prevents local focal degeneracy. Global tube self-intersections must be checked separately.\n\n---\n\n# 3. Local mean curvature\n\nFor the outward unit radial normal, define \\(H\\) as the sum of the three principal curvatures.\n\nThen\n\n\\[\nH=\\nabla\\cdot n.\n\\]\n\nUsing the tubular volume element,\n\n\\[\nH=\n\\frac{1}{hr^2}\n\\frac{\\partial}{\\partial r}\n(hr^2).\n\\]\n\nDifferentiation gives\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nH=\n\\frac2r-\n\\frac{\\kappa\\cos\\theta}\n{1-\\kappa r\\cos\\theta}.\n}\n\\tag{H-09}\n\\]\n\nThe result depends on the direction around the tube.\n\nAt fixed radius \\(R\\), introduce\n\n\\[\n\\alpha=\\kappa R.\n\\]\n\nThen\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nH(\\theta)=\n\\frac2R-\n\\frac{\\kappa\\cos\\theta}\n{1-\\alpha\\cos\\theta}.\n}\n\\tag{H-10}\n\\]\n\nThe torsion \\(\\tau\\) does not appear in this local scalar.\n\nThat does not mean torsion is physically irrelevant. It means this particular geometric invariant does not detect it.\n\n---\n\n# 4. Exact integrated invariants\n\nThe surface measure per unit centerline arclength is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\ndA=\n(1-\\alpha\\cos\\theta)\nR^2\\sin\\theta\\,d\\theta\\,d\\phi.\n}\n\\tag{H-11}\n\\]\n\n## Area\n\nIntegrating over the normal two-sphere,\n\n\\[\nA=\n2\\pi R^2\n\\int_{-1}^{1}(1-\\alpha\\mu)\\,d\\mu,\n\\]\n\nwhere\n\n\\[\n\\mu=\\cos\\theta.\n\\]\n\nThe odd term integrates to zero.\n\nTherefore,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{A=4\\pi R^2.}\n\\tag{H-12}\n\\]\n\n## Integrated mean curvature\n\nMultiply \\(H\\) by the area density:\n\n\\[\nH\\,dA\n=\n\\left(\n2R-3\\kappa R^2\\mu\n\\right)d\\Omega.\n\\]\n\nAgain, the odd term integrates to zero.\n\nThus\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\int H\\,dA=8\\pi R.\n}\n\\tag{H-13}\n\\]\n\n## Integrated squared curvature\n\nNow consider\n\n\\[\nI_2=\\int H^2\\,dA.\n\\]\n\nSubstitution gives\n\n\\[\nI_2=\n2\\pi\n\\int_{-1}^{1}\n\\frac{(2-3\\alpha\\mu)^2}\n{1-\\alpha\\mu}\n\\,d\\mu.\n\\]\n\nAn antiderivative of the integrand is\n\n\\[\n3\\mu-\n\\frac92\\alpha\\mu^2-\n\\frac1\\alpha\\ln(1-\\alpha\\mu).\n\\]\n\nFor\n\n\\[\n0<\\alpha<1,\n\\]\n\nthe logarithms remain real throughout the integration interval.\n\nEvaluating the endpoints gives\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nI_2=\n12\\pi+\n\\frac{2\\pi}{\\alpha}\n\\ln\\left(\n\\frac{1+\\alpha}{1-\\alpha}\n\\right).\n}\n\\tag{H-14}\n\\]\n\nIn the straight-core limit,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\lim_{\\alpha\\rightarrow0}I_2=16\\pi.\n}\n\\tag{H-15}\n\\]\n\nThe difference is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nI_2-16\\pi=\n4\\pi\n\\left(\n\\frac{\\operatorname{atanh}\\alpha}{\\alpha}-1\n\\right).\n}\n\\tag{H-16}\n\\]\n\nSince\n\n\\[\n\\frac{d}{d\\alpha}\n\\left(\n\\operatorname{atanh}\\alpha-\\alpha\n\\right)\n=\n\\frac{\\alpha^2}{1-\\alpha^2}>0,\n\\]\n\nthe excess is strictly positive for \\(0<\\alpha<1\\).\n\n**This gives an exact curvature-sensitive geometric quantity without introducing an empirical coefficient.**\n\n---\n\n# 5. Finite-thickness shell and conserved current\n\nDefine a radial shell profile\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\ng_D(r)=\n\\frac{15}{16D}\n\\left[\n1-\\left(\\frac{r-R}{D}\\right)^2\n\\right]^2\n}\n\\tag{H-17}\n\\]\n\ninside\n\n\\[\n|r-R|<D,\n\\]\n\nand zero elsewhere.\n\nRequire\n\n\\[\n0<D<R.\n\\]\n\nThe profile is \\(C^1\\) and satisfies\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\int g_D(r)\\,dr=1.\n}\n\\tag{H-18}\n\\]\n\nThe boundary's smooth Thom-type one-form is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\\eta=g_D(r)\\,dr.}\n\\tag{H-19}\n\\]\n\nIt is closed:\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{d\\eta=0.}\n\\]\n\nIts Hodge dual is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n*\\eta=\n-g_Dhr^2\\sin\\theta\\,\nds\\wedge d\\theta\\wedge d\\phi.\n}\n\\tag{H-20}\n\\]\n\nConsequently,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nd(*\\eta)=\n\\partial_r(g_Dhr^2)\n\\sin\\theta\\,\nds\\wedge dr\\wedge d\\theta\\wedge d\\phi.\n}\n\\tag{H-21}\n\\]\n\nAt the shell's central radius,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\left.\n\\partial_r(g_Dhr^2)\n\\right|_R\n=\n\\frac{15}{16D}\n\\left(\n2R-3\\kappa R^2\\cos\\theta\n\\right).\n}\n\\tag{H-22}\n\\]\n\nThis generally does not vanish.\n\nNow define the separate longitudinal three-form\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nJ=\n\\frac{g_D(r)}{4\\pi r^2}\n\\theta_1\\wedge\\theta_2\\wedge\\theta_3\n}\n\\tag{H-23}\n\\]\n\nusing the moving normal coframe\n\n\\[\n\\theta_1=du-\\tau v\\,ds,\n\\]\n\n\\[\n\\theta_2=dv+\\tau u\\,ds,\n\\]\n\n\\[\n\\theta_3=dw.\n\\]\n\nThe calculation gives\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{dJ=0}\n\\]\n\nand\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\\int_{B^3}J=1.}\n\\]\n\nThe pullback of \\(J\\) to the shell hypersurface \\(r=R\\) vanishes.\n\nThus the conserved longitudinal three-form is not the intrinsic three-dimensional boundary volume form, even though it is supported in the same finite-thickness shell.\n\n---\n\n# 6. Numerical verification\n\nThe executed test used\n\n\\[\na=2,\\quad b=1,\\quad R=0.4,\\quad D=0.1.\n\\]\n\nTherefore,\n\n\\[\n\\kappa=0.4,\\qquad\n\\tau=0.2,\\qquad\n\\alpha=0.16.\n\\]\n\nThe resulting integrals were:\n\n| Quantity | Computed value |\n|---|---:|\n| Surface area per unit arclength | 2.0106192982974678 |\n| Integrated mean curvature | 10.053096491487338 |\n| Integrated squared curvature | 50.37439331692574 |\n| Straight-core squared-curvature integral | 50.26548245743669 |\n| Curvature-dependent excess | **0.10891085948905044** |\n| Transverse shell flux | 0.9999999999999998 |\n| Maximum relative Jacobian error | \\(2.447\\times10^{-10}\\) |\n| Maximum extrinsic curvature error | \\(3.831\\times10^{-7}\\) |\n\nThe independent extrinsic-curvature test reconstructed the shape operator directly from finite differences of the embedded four-dimensional surface.\n\nIt did not use equation H-09 to calculate the comparison values.\n\nThe numerical and symbolic checks agreed within the stated numerical tolerances.\n\n### Class P precision geometry\n\n![Helical shell local curvature and straight-core comparison](sandbox:/mnt/data/hale_s4_20261020/classP_helical_shell_curvature.png)\n\nThe plotted local curvature varies with normal direction.\n\nThe global cancellation in equations H-12 and H-13 occurs despite that variation.\n\n---\n\n# 7. Complete executed Python\n\nThe full script, including symbolic identities, numerical tests and precision visualization, is preserved here:\n\n**[Download executable Hale recovered physical SAT equations.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\nimport sympy as S\nimport numpy as np\nfrom scipy.integrate import quad\n\nimport matplotlib\nmatplotlib.use(\"Agg\")\nimport
