r)/(h*r*r)\n)\n\nassert S.simplify(\n    H-(2/r-k*mu/h)\n) == 0\n\nA = S.simplify(\n    2*pi*R**2*S.integrate(\n        1-k*R*mu,\n        (mu,-1,1)\n    )\n)\n\nM = S.simplify(\n    2*pi*R**2*S.integrate(\n        (H*h).subs(r,R),\n        (mu,-1,1)\n    )\n)\n\nassert A == 4*pi*R**2\nassert M == 8*pi*R\n\nalpha = S.symbols(\n    \"alpha\", real=True, positive=True\n)\n\nH2_integrand = S.cancel(\n    (H*H*h*r*r).subs({\n        r:R,\n        k:alpha/R\n    })\n)\n\nH2_primitive = (\n    3*mu\n    - S.Rational(9,2)*alpha*mu**2\n    - S.log(1-alpha*mu)/alpha\n)\n\nassert S.simplify(\n    S.diff(H2_primitive,mu)\n    - H2_integrand\n) == 0\n\nH2_closed = (\n    12*pi\n    + 2*pi/alpha\n    * S.log((1+alpha)/(1-alpha))\n)\n\nassert S.simplify(\n    H2_integrand\n    -(2-3*alpha*mu)**2/(1-alpha*mu)\n) == 0\n\nassert S.limit(\n    H2_closed,alpha,0,dir=\"+\"\n) == 16*pi\n\nassert S.simplify(\n    S.diff(\n        S.atanh(alpha)-alpha,\n        alpha\n    )\n    -alpha**2/(1-alpha**2)\n) == 0\n\nz = S.symbols(\"z\",real=True)\n\ngz = (\n    S.Rational(15,16)/D\n    *(1-z*z)**2\n)\n\ng = gz.subs(z,(r-R)/D)\n\nassert S.simplify(\n    S.integrate(\n        gz*D,(z,-1,1)\n    )\n) == 1\n\nassert S.simplify(\n    S.diff(g,r).subs(r,R)\n) == 0\n\nfor edge in (R-D,R+D):\n    assert S.simplify(\n        g.subs(r,edge)\n    ) == 0\n\n    assert S.simplify(\n        S.diff(g,r).subs(r,edge)\n    ) == 0\n\nPDmid = S.factor(\n    S.diff(g*h*r*r,r).subs(r,R)\n)\n\nassert S.simplify(\n    PDmid\n    -S.Rational(15,16)/D\n    *(2*R-3*k*R*R*mu)\n) == 0\n\nf = g/(4*pi*r*r)\n\nflux = S.simplify(\n    S.integrate(\n        gz*D,(z,-1,1)\n    )\n)\n\nassert flux == 1\n\nu,v,w = S.symbols(\n    \"u v w\",real=True\n)\n\nr2 = u*u+v*v+w*w\nF = S.Function(\"F\")\n\nradial_zero = S.simplify(\n    t*(\n        v*S.diff(F(r2),u)\n        -u*S.diff(F(r2),v)\n    )\n)\n\nassert radial_zero == 0\n\nTh,Ph = S.symbols(\n    \"Th Ph\",real=True\n)\n\nuf = R*S.cos(Th)\nvf = R*S.sin(Th)*S.cos(Ph)\nwf = R*S.sin(Th)*S.sin(Ph)\n\ndef wedge_angular(a,b):\n    return (\n        S.diff(a,Th)*S.diff(b,Ph)\n        -S.diff(a,Ph)*S.diff(b,Th)\n    )\n\npullback_coeff = S.trigsimp(\n    -t*(\n        uf*wedge_angular(uf,wf)\n        +vf*wedge_angular(vf,wf)\n    )\n)\n\nassert S.simplify(\n    pullback_coeff\n) == 0\n\nG = S.Matrix([\n    [\n        h*h+t*t*(u*u+v*v),\n        -t*v,t*u,0\n    ],\n    [-t*v,1,0,0],\n    [t*u,0,1,0],\n    [0,0,0,1]\n])\n\nassert S.simplify(\n    G.det()-h*h\n) == 0\n\naval,bval = 2.,1.\nL = np.hypot(aval,bval)\n\nkap = aval/L**2\ntau = bval/L**2\n\nRv = .4\nDv = .1\n\nassert kap*(Rv+Dv)<1\n\ndef X(s0,r0,theta,phi):\n    p=s0/L\n\n    c,si=np.cos(p),np.sin(p)\n\n    gamma=np.array([\n        aval*c,\n        aval*si,\n        bval*p,\n        0.\n    ])\n\n    N=np.array([\n        -c,-si,0.,0.\n    ])\n\n    B=np.array([\n        bval/L*si,\n        -bval/L*c,\n        aval/L,\n        0.\n    ])\n\n    E=np.array([\n        0.,0.,0.,1.\n    ])\n\n    n=(\n        np.cos(theta)*N\n        +np.sin(theta)*np.cos(phi)*B\n        +np.sin(theta)*np.sin(phi)*E\n    )\n\n    return gamma+r0*n\n\ndef finite_jac(\n    s0,r0,theta,phi,\n    step=1e-5\n):\n    z=np.array([\n        s0,r0,theta,phi\n    ])\n\n    cols=[]\n\n    for i in range(4):\n        zp=z.copy()\n        zm=z.copy()\n\n        zp[i]+=step\n        zm[i]-=step\n\n        cols.append(\n            (X(*zp)-X(*zm))/(2*step)\n        )\n\n    return np.column_stack(cols)\n\nrng=np.random.default_rng(\n    202610200948\n)\n\nmax_jac_relerr=0.\nmax_orthogonality=0.\n\nfor _ in range(100):\n    s0=float(rng.uniform(-10,10))\n    r0=float(\n        rng.uniform(Rv-Dv,Rv+Dv)\n    )\n    theta=float(\n        rng.uniform(.1,np.pi-.1)\n    )\n    phi=float(\n        rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)\n    )\n\n    J=finite_jac(\n        s0,r0,theta,phi\n    )\n\n    det=np.linalg.det(J)\n\n    target=(\n        (1-kap*r0*np.cos(theta))\n        *r0*r0*np.sin(theta)\n    )\n\n    err=abs(\n        abs(det)-target\n    )/target\n\n    max_jac_relerr=max(\n        max_jac_relerr,err\n    )\n\n    assert err<2e-9\n\n    err=max(\n        abs(np.dot(J[:,1],J[:,i]))\n        for i in (0,2,3)\n    )\n\n    max_orthogonality=max(\n        max_orthogonality,err\n    )\n\n    assert err<3e-9\n\ndef extrinsic_H(\n    s0,theta,phi,\n    step=2e-4\n):\n    base=np.array([\n        s0,Rv,theta,phi\n    ])\n\n    ids=(0,2,3)\n    X0=X(*base)\n\n    dr=np.array([\n        0.,step,0.,0.\n    ])\n\n    n=(\n        X(*(base+dr))\n        -X(*(base-dr))\n    )/(2*step)\n\n    T=np.empty((4,3))\n    B2=np.empty((3,3))\n\n    for j,idx in enumerate(ids):\n        ej=np.zeros(4)\n        ej[idx]=step\n\n        T[:,j]=(\n            X(*(base+ej))\n            -X(*(base-ej))\n        )/(2*step)\n\n        B2[j,j]=-np.dot(\n            n,\n            (\n                X(*(base+ej))\n                -2*X0\n                +X(*(base-ej))\n            )/step**2\n        )\n\n        for k2,idx2 in enumerate(\n            ids[:j]\n        ):\n            ek=np.zeros(4)\n            ek[idx2]=step\n\n            d2=(\n                X(*(base+ej+ek))\n                -X(*(base+ej-ek))\n                -X(*(base-ej+ek))\n                +X(*(base-ej-ek))\n            )/(4*step**2)\n\n            B2[j,k2]=B2[k2,j]=(\n                -np.dot(n,d2)\n            )\n\n    G2=T.T@T\n\n    return np.trace(\n        np.linalg.solve(G2,B2)\n    )\n\nmax_shape_err=0.\n\nfor _ in range(24):\n    s0=float(\n        rng.uniform(-7,7)\n    )\n\n    th=float(\n        rng.uniform(.2,np.pi-.2)\n    )\n\n    ph=float(\n        rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)\n    )\n\n    h_ext=extrinsic_H(\n        s0,th,ph\n    )\n\n    h_analytic=(\n        2/Rv\n        -kap*np.cos(th)\n        /(1-kap*Rv*np.cos(th))\n    )\n\n    max_shape_err=max(\n        max_shape_err,\n        abs(h_ext-h_analytic)\n    )\n\n    assert abs(\n        h_ext-h_analytic\n    )<2e-6\n\ndef Hnum(mu):\n    return (\n        2/Rv\n        -kap*mu/(1-kap*Rv*mu)\n    )\n\ndef dA(mu):\n    return (\n        2*np.pi*Rv*Rv\n        *(1-kap*Rv*mu)\n    )\n\narea,area_err=quad(\n    dA,-1,1,epsabs=1e-12\n)\n\nmean,mean_err=quad(\n    lambda mu:Hnum(mu)*dA(mu),\n    -1,1,epsabs=1e-12\n)\n\nsq,sq_err=quad(\n    lambda mu:Hnum(mu)**2*dA(mu),\n    -1,1,epsabs=1e-12\n)\n\nclosed_sq=float(\n    H2_closed.subs(\n        alpha,kap*Rv\n    )\n)\n\nassert abs(\n    area-4*np.pi*Rv*Rv\n)<1e-12\n\nassert abs(\n    mean-8*np.pi*Rv\n)<1e-12\n\nassert abs(\n    sq-closed_sq\n)<1e-12\n\nassert The third does not follow from the stated correction.\n\nThis changes the size of the previously discussed mass-ratio discrepancy.\n\nRather than approximately 0.57%, the corrected stabilized baseline lies **1.106% below CODATA**.\n\nThe quoted Holonomy Bridge is approximately **136 times too small** to close the corrected gap.\n\nImportantly, the archive already contains an explicit rejection of this bridge as an insufficient correction. `6b0d229108186861bda48c54472cacb60afd9dc5`  \n**Length:** 20,038 characters, 640 lines.\n\nThe historical calculator preserves:\n\n```python\n@property\ndef B0(self) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Pure None:\n        return self.B_override\n    return self.B0 * (1.0 + self.delta_lattice)\n\n@property\ndef proton_electron_ratio_v1(self) -> float:\n    \"\"\"mu = 3/(2*B^5).\"\"\"\n    return 3.0 / (2.0 * self.B_eff**5)\n```\n\nThe default configuration sets\n\n```python\ndelta_lattice = 0.0\nB_override = None\n```\n\nThus, absent an explicit override, this historical implementation uses \\(B_0\\).\n\nThe algebraic formula is preserved consistently between the two archives.\n\n**Genealogy limitation:** These are surviving source copies. Their blob hashes establish exact content identity, not original authorship or composition dates. The earliest occurrence of the fifth-power derivation remains unidentified.\n\n---\n\n## 2. A separate geometrical correction\n\nThe SAT source describes\n\n\\[\nB_0=\\frac{3}{4\\pi}\n\\]\n\nas the ratio of the volume of a unit three-ball to the surface area of its bounding two-sphere.\n\nThat geometrical identification is incorrect.\n\nFor a unit three-ball,\n\n\\[\nV_3=\\frac{4\\pi}{3}.\n\\]\n\nIts boundary area is\n\n\\[\nA_2=4\\pi.\n\\]\n\nTherefore,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\\frac{V_3}{A_2}=\\frac13.}\n\\]\n\nBut\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\frac{1}{V_3}=\\frac{3}{4\\pi}.\n}\n\\]\n\nThus \\(B_0\\) is the **reciprocal unit three-ball volume**, not its volume-to-area ratio.\n\nThis is a uniquely demonstrable correction to the verbal geometrical identification.\n\nIt does not authorize replacing the historical numerical definition of \\(B\\).\n\nFor a sphere of general radius \\(r\\),\n\n\\[\n\\frac{V_3(r)}{A_2(r)}=\\frac r3,\n\\]\n\nwhich also shows why a length normalization must be specified before treating that ratio as dimensionless.\n\nThe numerical value \\(3/(4\\pi)\\) remains an exact mathematical quantity. Its proposed physical interpretation requires separate derivation.\n\n---\n\n## 3. CODATA comparison\n\nThe entity[\"organization\",\"National Institute of Standards and Technology\",\"United States measurement standards agency\"] publishes the 2022 entity[\"organization\",\"CODATA\",\"Committee on Data of the International Science Council\"] recommended proton-electron mass ratio:\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\mu_{\\rm CODATA}\n=\n1836.152673426(32).\n}\n\\]\n\nThe standard uncertainty is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\sigma_\\mu=0.000000032.\n}\n\\]\n\nThe quantity is dimensionless. The 2022 adjustment remains the published reference used here. citeturn0search5turn0search0\n\n### Direct calculation\n\nFor the original geometrical value,\n\n\\[\nB_0=0.23873241463784300365\\ldots\n\\]\n\nwe obtain\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\mu(B_0)=1934.34664950696424778\\ldots\n}\n\\]\n\nThe residual is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\Delta\\mu_0=+98.19397608096425.\n}\n\\]\n\nRelative discrepancy:\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{+5.347811078\\%.}\n\\]\n\nFor the stabilized value,\n\n\\[\nB_{\\rm stable}=0.24177,\n\\]\n\nwe obtain\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\mu(B_{\\rm stable})\n=\n1815.84680658617078081\\ldots\n}\n\\]\n\nThe residual is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\Delta\\mu_{\\rm stable}\n=\n-20.30586683982922.\n}\n\\]\n\nRelative discrepancy:\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{-1.105892072\\%.}\n\\]\n\nFinally, apply the stated bridge:\n\n\\[\n\\mu_{\\rm bridge}\n=\n1815.84680658617(1+0.000082).\n\\]\n\nThis gives\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\mu_{\\rm bridge}\n=\n1815.99570602431084682\\ldots\n}\n\\]\n\nThe remaining residual is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\Delta\\mu_{\\rm bridge}\n=\n-20.15696740168915.\n}\n\\]\n\nRelative discrepancy:\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{-1.097782755\\%.}\n\\]\n\nThe stated correction does not close the gap.\n\n### What bridge would be required?\n\nSolving algebraically,\n\n\\[\n\\mu_{\\rm CODATA}\n=\n\\frac{3}{2B_{\\rm stable}^5}\n(1+\\Delta_{\\rm required})\n\\]\n\ngives\n\n\\[\n\\Delta_{\\rm required}\n=\n\\frac{\\mu_{\\rm CODATA}}\n{\\mu(B_{\\rm stable})}-1.\n\\]\n\nTherefore,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\Delta_{\\rm required}\n=\n0.01118258807195562113\\ldots\n}\n\\]\n\nCompare this with\n\n\\[\n\\Delta_{\\rm stated}=0.000082.\n\\]\n\nTheir ratio is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\frac{\\Delta_{\\rm required}}\n{\\Delta_{\\rm stated}}\n=\n136.37302526775\\ldots\n}\n\\]\n\nThis required correction is calculated **from the measured mass ratio**.\n\nIt is a fitted value, not an independent prediction.\n\nNo such correction has been adopted.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Parameter sensitivity\n\nDifferent values of \\(B\\) produce substantially different mass ratios because the relationship contains a fifth power.\n\nDifferentiate:\n\n\\[\n\\mu(B)=\\frac{3}{2}B^{-5}.\n\\]\n\nThen\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\frac{d\\mu}{dB}\n=\n-\\frac{15}{2B^6}\n=\n-\\frac{5\\mu}{B}.\n}\n\\]\n\nIts logarithmic sensitivity is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\frac{d\\ln\\mu}{d\\ln B}=-5.\n}\n\\]\n\nA small fractional increase in \\(B\\) therefore produces approximately five times that fractional decrease in \\(\\mu\\).\n\nThis matters when evaluating whether a proposed stabilization is derived or tuned.\n\nSolving directly for the \\(B\\) that reproduces CODATA gives\n\n\\[\nB_{\\rm fit}\n=\n\\left(\n\\frac{3}{2\\mu_{\\rm CODATA}}\n\\right)^{1/5}.\n\\]\n\nNumerically,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nB_{\\rm fit}\n=\n0.24123287565696145759\\ldots\n}\n\\]\n\nWith the quoted bridge held fixed, the fitted value becomes\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nB_{\\rm fit,bridge}\n=\n0.24123683174636462731\\ldots\n}\n\\]\n\nNeither value is independently derived here.\n\nThe difference between these fitted values and \\(B_{\\rm stable}\\) is a constraint for future geometrical reconstruction, not permission to substitute a convenient number.\n\n### Class P numerical geometry\n\n![Historical projection constant and proton-electron mass ratio](sandbox:/mnt/data/morrow_s4_20261020/classP_mass_ratio.png)\n\nThe curve is the exact historical fifth-power relationship.\n\nThe intersection with the CODATA line determines a fitted \\(B\\). Complete executed Python audit\n\nThe following script was actually executed.\n\nIt uses SymPy for symbolic identities and limits, and mpmath at 55 decimal digits for the numerical comparisons.\n\n```python\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"CALC-S4-20261020T1048Z-MORROW-01\n\nExact recovery check, not a physical SAT derivation.\n\nHistorical:\nSCALING.txt SAT archive\nblob dcf23fbeda258da813f54360ab1a7f33243c7089\n\nMACHINE1.py HsH archive\nblob 6b0d229108186861bda48c54472cacb60afd9dc5\n\nCODATA 2022:\nm_p/m_e = 1836.152673426(32)\n\"\"\"\n\nimport sympy as s\nfrom mpmath import mp\n\nmp.dps = 55\n\npi = mp.pi\n\nB0 = 3 / (4*pi)\nBs = mp.mpf('0.24177')\ndelta = mp.mpf('0.000082')\n\nmu_obs = mp.mpf('1836.152673426')\nsigma = mp.mpf('0.000000032')\n\nmu = lambda B: mp.mpf(3)/(2*B**5)\n\nbase = mu(B0)\nstable = mu(Bs)\nbridged = stable*(1+delta)\n\nrequired = mu_obs/stable-1\n\nBfit = (\n    mp.mpf(3)/(2*mu_obs)\n)**(mp.mpf(1)/5)\n\nBfit_bridge = (\n    mp.mpf(3)*(1+delta)/(2*mu_obs)\n)**(mp.mpf(1)/5)\n\nV3 = 4*pi/3\nA2 = 4*pi\n\n# Euclidean geometrical identity\nassert mp.almosteq(\n    V3/A2,\n    mp.mpf(1)/3\n)\n\nassert mp.almosteq(\n    1/V3,\n    B0\n)\n\nassert not mp.almosteq(\n    V3/A2,\n    B0\n)\n\n# Exact symbolic checks\nB = s.symbols('B', positive=True)\n\nexpr = s.Rational(3,2)/B**5\n\nassert s.simplify(\n    s.diff(expr,B)+5*expr/B\n) == 0\n\nassert s.limit(\n    expr,B,0,dir='+'\n) == s.oo\n\nassert s.limit(\n    expr,B,s.oo\n) == 0\n\nassert s.simplify(\n    s.diff(expr,B)\n).is_negative is True\n\n# Equivalent expression when B=3/(4*pi)\nassert s.simplify(\n    expr.subs(\n        B,s.Rational(3,4)/s.pi\n    )\n    -\n    2*s.pi/(\n        s.Rational(3,4)/s.pi\n    )**4\n) == 0\n\n# Numerical discrepancy tests\nassert base > mu_obs > stable\nassert bridged < mu_obs\n\nassert abs(\n    bridged-mu_obs\n) > sigma\n\nassert abs(\n    required-delta\n) > mp.mpf('0.005')\n\n# Explicitly label these as inverse fits\nassert abs(\n    mu(Bfit)-mu_obs\n) < mp.mpf('1e-45')\n\nassert abs(\n    mu(Bfit_bridge)*(1+delta)-mu_obs\n) < mp.mpf('1e-45')\n\nfmt = lambda x: mp.nstr(x,28)\n\nprint(\n    'TASK CALC-S4-20261020T1048Z-MORROW-01'\n)\n\nprint(\n    'CODATA 2022 mu_obs=',\n    fmt(mu_obs),\n    'sigma=',\n    fmt(sigma)\n)\n\nprint(\n    'Euclidean V3=',\n    fmt(V3),\n    'A2=',\n    fmt(A2)\n)\n\nprint(\n    'V3/A2=',\n    fmt(V3/A2),\n    '1/V3=',\n    fmt(1/V3),\n    'B0=',\n    fmt(B0)\n)\n\nprint('mu(B0)=',fmt(base))\nprint('mu(0.24177)=',fmt(stable))\n\nprint(\n    'mu(0.24177)*(1+8.2e-5)=',\n    fmt(bridged)\n)\n\nprint(\n    'required bridge delta for CODATA=',\n    fmt(required)\n)\n\nprint(\n    'ratio required / stated bridge=',\n    fmt(required/delta)\n)\n\nprint(\n    'B fitted directly to CODATA=',\n    fmt(Bfit)\n)\n\nprint(\n    'B fitted with fixed bridge=',\n    fmt(Bfit_bridge)\n)\n\nfor label,value in [\n    ('base',base),\n    ('stable',stable),\n    ('stated_bridge',bridged)\n]:\n    res = value-mu_obs\n\n    print(\n        label,\n        'residual=',fmt(res),\n        'relative_percent=',\n        fmt(100*res/mu_obs),\n        'CODATA_sigma_units_formal=',\n        fmt(res/sigma)\n    )\n\nprint(\n    'sensitivity dmu/dB=-5mu/B, '\n    'logarithmic elasticity=-5'\n)\n\nprint(\n    'No predictive uncertainty assigned to B; '\n    'sigma residual is reference-only.'\n)\n\nprint(\n    'RESULT: historical numerical CODATA snap '\n    'REJECTED; physical prediction UNRESOLVED'\n)\n\nprint('ALL_ASSERTIONS_PASSED')\n```\n\n### Actual execution results\n\n```text\nTASK CALC-S4-20261020T1048Z-MORROW-01\n\nCODATA 2022 This investigation tests that formula against another equation preserved in the same HsH calculator: the proposed geometrical expression for the reduced Planck constant.\n\nThe two archived equations are\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\\mu=\\frac{3}{2B^5}}\n\\tag{O-01}\n\\]\n\nand\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\\hbar=m_0c\\ell_fB.}\n\\tag{O-02}\n\\]\n\nThese imply an exact identity:\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\mu\\hbar^5\n=\n\\frac32(m_0c\\ell_f)^5.\n}\n\\tag{O-03}\n\\]\n\n**The projection parameter \\(B\\) disappears.**\n\nThis is important because adjusting \\(B\\) cannot independently repair both equations once \\(m_0\\) and \\(\\ell_f\\) are fixed.\n\nFor the historical candidate \\(\\ell_f=0.7937\\) fm, with \\(m_0\\) identified as the electron mass, the joint equations miss the observed reduced Planck constant by a factor of approximately **2,017**, even when \\(B\\) is fitted to reproduce the proton/electron mass ratio exactly.\n\nThis is a conditional inconsistency in the recovered calculator, not a rejection of SAT/H(s)H or a determination that the historical geometrical scale must be abandoned.\n\n### Verification summary\n\n| Component | Result |\n|---|---|\n| Historical equations recovered from SAT and HsH | Yes |\n| Exact elimination of \\(B\\) | `INTERNALLY_CHECKED` |\n| Dimensional consistency | Passed |\n| Algebraic signs and limits | Passed |\n| Historical calculator defaults | Reproduced |\n| Proton/electron mass ratio | `CODATA_COMPARED` |\n| Reduced Planck constant | `CODATA_COMPARED` |\n| Historical fine-structure expression | `CODATA_COMPARED` |\n| Literal joint closure at stated fixed scales | `REJECTED` |\n| Alternative physical scale identification | `UNRESOLVED` |\n| BEDROCK promotion | None |\n| Publication | `SUBMISSION_ROUTE_UNAVAILABLE` |\n\n---\n\n## 1. Historical mathematics recovered\n\n### SAT archive\n\nurlSCALING.txthttps://github.com/Satobloc/SAT_THEORY_ARCHIVE_2023-25/blob/main/SCALING.txt\n\n**Git blob:** `dcf23fbeda258da813f54360ab1a7f33243c7089`  \n**Length:** 22,550 characters, 457 lines  \n**Relevant passages:** lines 25–55.\n\nThis source preserves\n\n\\[\nB_0=\\frac{3}{4\\pi},\n\\]\n\n\\[\nB_{\\rm stable}\\approx0.24177,\n\\]\n\nand\n\n\\[\n\\mu=\\frac{3}{2B^5}.\n\\]\n\nIt also discusses a candidate scale\n\n\\[\n\\ell_f\\approx0.7937\\ \\mathrm{fm}.\n\\]\n\nUnder current Nathan Direct clarification, this historical length generally denotes the radius of a first-order helical coil, **not the intrinsic filament thickness**.\n\nThe document's original composition date remains unauthenticated.\n\n### HsH archive\n\nurlMACHINE1.pyhttps://github.com/Satobloc/HsH/blob/main/DEVELOPMENT_FULL_CONVOS/MOSTLY_EARLY_NLM/MACHINE1.py\n\n**Git blob:** `6b0d229108186861bda48c54472cacb60afd9dc5`  \n**Length:** 20,038 characters, 640 lines.\n\nThis is an archived Python generator that writes a nested `sat_model.py` script.\n\nThe relevant source locations are:\n\n| Lines | Historical quantity |\n|---|---|\n| 48 | Master length \\(\\ell_f\\) |\n| 67–76 | Mass and projection inputs |\n| 141–150 | \\(B_0\\) and \\(B_{\\rm eff}\\) |\n| 210–212 | Reduced Planck constant |\n| 282–299 | Two fine-structure expressions |\n| 413–415 | Proton/electron mass ratio |\n\nThe exact historical expressions are:\n\n```python\nreturn 3.0 / (4.0 * PI)\n\nreturn self.B0 * (1.0 + self.delta_lattice)\n\nreturn 3.0 / (2.0 * self.B_eff**5)\n\nreturn self.m0 * self.c * self.ell_f * self.B_eff\n\nreturn 1.0 / (\n    self.B0**3 * 1.5 * (1.0 - Their earliest original-message occurrence and authorship have not yet been authenticated.\n\n---\n\n# 2. Exact algebraic reconstruction\n\nStarting with\n\n\\[\n\\mu=\\frac{3}{2B^5},\n\\]\n\nmultiply by\n\n\\[\n\\hbar^5=(m_0c\\ell_fB)^5.\n\\]\n\nThen\n\n\\[\n\\mu\\hbar^5\n=\n\\frac{3}{2B^5}\n(m_0c\\ell_f)^5B^5.\n\\]\n\nCancel \\(B^5\\):\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\mu\\hbar^5\n=\n\\frac32(m_0c\\ell_f)^5.\n}\n\\]\n\nThis identity is exact for the two historical equations as written.\n\nIt is independent of the value assigned to \\(B\\).\n\n### Dimensional check\n\nThe proposed reduced Planck constant has dimensions\n\n\\[\n[m_0c\\ell_f]\n=\n\\mathrm{kg}\\frac{\\mathrm m}{\\mathrm s}\\mathrm m\n=\n\\mathrm{J\\,s}.\n\\]\n\nThus equation O-02 is dimensionally homogeneous.\n\nEquation O-01 is dimensionless.\n\nEquation O-03 is therefore dimensionally consistent.\n\n### Limiting cases\n\nFor \\(B>0\\),\n\n\\[\n\\frac{d\\mu}{dB}\n=\n-\\frac{15}{2B^6}<0.\n\\]\n\nMeanwhile,\n\n\\[\n\\frac{\\partial\\hbar}{\\partial B}\n=\nm_0c\\ell_f>0.\n\\]\n\nIncreasing \\(B\\) decreases the mass ratio but increases the proposed reduced Planck constant.\n\nThe product relation O-03 remains unchanged.\n\nThese properties were checked symbolically with SymPy.\n\n---\n\n# 3. CODATA 2022 comparison\n\nThe reference values come from the entity[\"organization\",\"National Institute of Standards and Technology\",\"United States measurement standards institute\"] and the 2022 entity[\"organization\",\"CODATA\",\"Committee on Data of the International Science Council\"] adjustment.\n\nurlNIST CODATA 2022 reference tablesturn3search0\n\nThe relevant quantities are\n\n\\[\n\\mu_{\\rm CODATA}=1836.152673426(32),\n\\]\n\n\\[\nm_e=9.1093837139(28)\\times10^{-31}\\ \\mathrm{kg},\n\\]\n\n\\[\n\\alpha=0.0072973525643(11),\n\\]\n\nand\n\n\\[\n\\hbar=\\frac{6.62607015\\times10^{-34}}{2\\pi}\n\\ \\mathrm{J\\,s}.\n\\]\n\nThe Planck constant is exact under the present SI definition. The electron mass, mass ratio and fine-structure constant have measurement uncertainties. citeturn3search0turn2search10turn0search3\n\n## Test A: Fit the mass ratio\n\nInvert the historical formula:\n\n\\[\nB_{\\mu}\n=\n\\left(\n\\frac{3}{2\\mu_{\\rm CODATA}}\n\\right)^{1/5}.\n\\]\n\nThe calculated result Independent fine-structure expression\n\nThe archived calculator also contains\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\alpha^{-1}\n\\approx\n\\frac{1}\n{B_0^3(3/2)(1-B_0/2)}.\n}\n\\tag{O-11}\n\\]\n\nUsing\n\n\\[\nB_0=\\frac{3}{4\\pi},\n\\]\n\nthe calculation gives\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\alpha_{\\rm series}\n=\n0.0179730084226806401763.\n}\n\\]\n\nEquivalently,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\alpha_{\\rm series}^{-1}\n=\n55.63898800259127088.\n}\n\\]\n\nThe CODATA inverse fine-structure constant is approximately 137.036.\n\nThe historical expression therefore differs by\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{+146.2949167\\%}\n\\]\n\nin \\(\\alpha\\).\n\nCrucially, equation O-11 uses \\(B_0\\), not \\(\\ell_f\\).\n\n**Changing the master length cannot correct this particular expression.**\n\nThe calculator's other fine-structure expression,\n\n\\[\n\\alpha_{\\rm standard}\n=\n\\frac{e^2}{4\\pi\\epsilon_0\\hbar c},\n\\]\n\ndepends directly on its computed \\(\\hbar\\).\n\nIts discrepancy is therefore algebraically coupled to the Planck-constant discrepancy. Executed Python audit\n\nThe complete audit was executed with SymPy and mpmath at 65-digit precision.\n\n```python\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"CALC-S4-20261020T1148Z-ORSON-VAY-01.\n\nVerbatim historical equations from Satobloc/HsH:\n  DEVELOPMENT_FULL_CONVOS/MOSTLY_EARLY_NLM/MACHINE1.py\n  git blob 6b0d229108186861bda48c54472cacb60afd9dc5\n  B0 = 3/(4*pi)\n  B_eff = B0*(1+delta_lattice), unless overridden\n  mu = 3/(2*B_eff**5)\n  hbar = m0*c*ell_f*B_eff\n  alpha_inverse_power_series = 1/(B0**3 * (3/2)*(1-B0/2))\n  alpha_standard = e**2/(4*pi*epsilon0*hbar*c)\n\nContextual ell_f ~0.7937 fm is a historical coil-radius candidate, not\nfilament thickness and not experimentally measured. This is a conditional\nconsistency audit of the *particular* calculator's shared ell_f identification.\nNo physical SAT/HsH theorem is claimed or revised.\n\"\"\"\nimport sympy as sp\nfrom mpmath import mp\nmp.dps = 65\nD = mp.mpf\npi = mp.pi\nc = D('299792458')                 # SI exact\nh = D('6.62607015e-34')           # SI exact\nhbar_obs = h/(2*pi)               # exact derived quantity\nme22 = D('9.1093837139e-31')     # CODATA 2022 input\ns_me22 = D('0.0000000028e-31')\nmp22 = D('1.67262192595e-27')\nmu22 = D('1836.152673426')\ns_mu22 = D('0.000000032')\nalpha22 = D('0.0072973525643')\ns_alpha22 = D('0.0000000000011')\ne = D('1.602176634e-19')         # SI exact\neps0_22 = D('8.8541878188e-12')\neps0_code = D('8.8541878128e-12')\nme_code = D('9.1093837015e-31')\nell_default = D('1e-15')\nell_candidate = D('0.7937e-15')\nB0 = 3/(4*pi)\nBs = D('0.24177')\nBmu = (D('3')/(2*mu22))**(D(1)/5)\nmu = lambda B: D('3')/(2*B**5)\nhbar = lambda B,ell,m: m*c*ell*B\nalpha_series = B0**3 * D('1.5') * (1-B0/2)\nalpha_formula = lambda hb, eps: e**2/(4*pi*eps*hb*c)\n\n# Symbolic exact cross-constraint, algebra and dimensionality.\nB, m, cc, ell =             "# Calculation Relay :48 | Alberr → Mira\n**20 October 2026, 08:48 Eastern**  \n**Task:** `CALC-S4-20261020T1248Z-ALBERR-01`\n\nPython verified the rotation:\n\n\\[\nh=228,\\qquad seq=5(228)+4=1144\n\\]\n\n\\[\n1144\\bmod19=4.\n\\]\n\n**Worker:** Alberr  \n**Next:** Mira\n\n## 🔬 Principal finding: A conservation-law error in the historical three-form construction\n\nThe SAT and H(s)H archives preserve an equation connecting a three-form worldvolume current to conservation:\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nJ_{\\mu\\nu\\rho}\\sim\n\\epsilon_{\\alpha\\mu\\nu\\rho}v^\\alpha\n}\n\\tag{A-01}\n\\]\n\nfollowed by\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nd^*J=0\n\\implies\n\\partial_\\mu J^\\mu=0.\n}\n\\tag{A-02}\n\\]\n\n**The second statement is incorrect when \\(J\\) is the three-form specified by the first.**\n\nThe uniquely determined correction is\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\ndJ=0\n\\iff\n\\partial_\\mu j^\\mu=0,\n\\qquad J=*j^\\flat.\n}\n\\tag{A-03}\n\\]\n\nThe distinction is consequential for finite-core worldtube geometry.\n\nA stationary, spatially localized tube current can satisfy the corrected conservation law while violating the archived condition \\(d^*J=0\\).\n\nThis was verified symbolically, including an explicit finite-core example.\n\n**Overall mathematical status:** `INTERNALLY_CHECKED`  \n**Historical conservation implication:** `REJECTED` as written  \n**Physical electromagnetic emergence:** `UNRESOLVED`\n\nThe underlying three-form construction is retained. The demonstrated error concerns the differential operator, not the decision to represent a worldline current by a three-form.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Historical recovery and genealogy\n\nThe original expression was recovered from both principal archives.\n\n| Source | Exact repository blob SHA | Relevant location |\n|---|---|---|\n| urlSAT: H(s)H MANIFOLDShttps://github.com/Satobloc/SAT_THEORY_ARCHIVE_2023-25/blob/main/H%28s%29H%20Dev%20%2B/H%28s%29H%20MANIFOLDS.txt | `54f2f530697ab6e9c44758ce3406c4144915c73d` | Approximately 890–894 |\n| urlHsH: F1 WHIRLIGIGGhttps://github.com/Satobloc/HsH/blob/main/DEVELOPMENT_FULL_CONVOS/MOSTLY_EARLY_NLM/F1%20WHIRLIGIGG.txt | `dcb0b820600b4b43593e4a225562efb724a2a43d` | Approximately 55–57 |\n| urlSAT PRE-H(s)H TIGHTENINGhttps://github.com/Satobloc/SAT_THEORY_ARCHIVE_2023-25/blob/main/SAT%20PRE-H%28s%29H%20TIGHTENING.txt | `054a76d76cd8a44b18fa4c545ece4e0e3f9f4e8e` | Approximately 3511–3527 |\n\nThe first SAT source explicitly presents the three-form current as a geometrical foundation for electromagnetism.\n\nThe HsH source repeats\n\n\\[\nJ_{\\mu\\nu\\rho}\\sim\n\\epsilon_{\\alpha\\mu\\nu\\rho}v^\\alpha,\n\\qquad d^*J=0.\n\\]\n\nThe third source describes the three-form transition as **IN PROGRESS**.\n\nThat historical qualification matters. The current was being developed as a proposed formalization, not presented as an independently completed mathematical derivation.\n\nThe surviving source blobs establish content identity. They do not establish the earliest original message, original authorship of each equation, or the precise development chronology.\n\nNo terminology change has been treated as supersession.\n\n---\n\n# 2. Exact reconstruction in Minkowski spacetime\n\nWork in four-dimensional entity[\"scientific_concept\",\"Minkowski spacetime\",\"flat spacetime of special relativity\"] with metric\n\n\\[\ng=\\operatorname{diag}(-1,+1,+1,+1)\n\\]\n\nand orientation\n\n\\[\n\\mathrm{vol}\n=\ndt\\wedge dx\\wedge dy\\wedge dz.\n\\]\n\nLet the vector current be\n\n\\[\nj=j^\\mu\\partial_\\mu.\n\\]\n\nIts metric-dual one-form is\n\n\\[\nj^\\flat=g_{\\mu\\nu}j^\\nu dx^\\mu.\n\\]\n\nDefine the three-form\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nJ=*j^\\flat=\\iota_j\\mathrm{vol}.\n}\n\\tag{A-04}\n\\]\n\nExplicitly,\n\n\\[\n\\begin{aligned}\nJ={}&j^t\\,dx\\wedge dy\\wedge dz\\\\\n&-j^x\\,dt\\wedge dy\\wedge dz\\\\\n&+j^y\\,dt\\wedge dx\\wedge dz\\\\\n&-j^z\\,dt\\wedge dx\\wedge dy.\n\\end{aligned}\n\\]\n\nExterior differentiation gives\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\ndJ=\n\\left(\n\\partial_tj^t+\n\\partial_xj^x+\n\\partial_yj^y+\n\\partial_zj^z\n\\right)\\mathrm{vol}.\n}\n\\tag{A-05}\n\\]\n\nTherefore,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\ndJ=0\n\\iff\n\\nabla_\\mu j^\\mu=0.\n}\n\\tag{A-06}\n\\]\n\nFor a flat coordinate chart, the divergence is the ordinary partial divergence.\n\n### What does \\(d(*J)\\) measure?\n\nIn the specified Lorentzian signature,\n\n\\[\n**=+1\n\\]\n\non one-forms.\n\nConsequently,\n\n\\[\n*J=j^\\flat\n\\]\n\nand\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nd(*J)=d(j^\\flat).\n}\n\\tag{A-07}\n\\]\n\nThis is a two-form containing antisymmetrized derivatives:\n\n\\[\nd(j^\\flat)\n=\n\\frac12\n\\left(\n\\partial_\\mu j_\\nu-\n\\partial_\\nu j_\\mu\n\\right)\ndx^\\mu\\wedge dx^\\nu.\n\\]\n\nIt measures curl-type structure, not divergence.\n\nThere is a notation ambiguity worth preserving: \\(d^*\\) can denote the codifferential rather than \\(d\\circ *\\). However, the vanishing of the codifferential of a three-form is equivalent to the vanishing of \\(d(*J)\\), because the Hodge star is invertible.\n\nNeither interpretation produces the claimed three-form conservation identity.\n\n---\n\n# 3. Two exact counterexamples\n\n## A. Conserved rotating current\n\nChoose\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nj=(0,-y,x,0).\n}\n\\]\n\nIts divergence is\n\n\\[\n\\partial_\\mu j^\\mu\n=\n\\partial_x(-y)+\\partial_y(x)=0.\n\\]\n\nTherefore,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{dJ=0.}\n\\]\n\nBut\n\n\\[\nj^\\flat=-y\\,dx+x\\,dy.\n\\]\n\nDifferentiating,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nd(*J)=2\\,dx\\wedge dy\\ne0.\n}\n\\]\n\nThus a conserved current need not satisfy the archived condition.\n\n### Class P precision visualization\n\n![Conserved rotational current](sandbox:/mnt/data/alberr_s4_20261020/classP_rotational_current.png)\n\nThe vector field rotates while retaining zero divergence.\n\nIts nonzero curl is not a violation of current conservation.\n\n## B. Curl-free but nonconserved current\n\nNow choose\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nj=(t,0,0,0).\n}\n\\]\n\nIts metric dual is\n\n\\[\nj^\\flat=-t\\,dt.\n\\]\n\nTherefore,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{d(*J)=0.}\n\\]\n\nBut\n\n\\[\n\\partial_\\mu j^\\mu=\\partial_t t=1.\n\\]\n\nConsequently,\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\ndJ=dt\\wedge dx\\wedge dy\\wedge dz\\ne0.\n}\n\\]\n\nThe archived condition is therefore not sufficient for conservation either.\n\n![Nonconserved time-expansion current](sandbox:/mnt/data/alberr_s4_20261020/classP_expansion_current.png)\n\nBoth counterexamples were executed using the same exterior-algebra implementation.\n\n---\n\n# 4. Finite-core worldtube minimal pair\n\nThis is the most directly relevant new calculation.\n\nConsider a stationary tube with current\n\n\\[\nj=\\rho(r)\\partial_t.\n\\]\n\nChoose the compact radial profile\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\rho(r)=\n\\begin{cases}\n\\left(1-\\dfrac{r^2}{R^2}\\right)^2,&r<R,\\\\[6pt]\n0,&r\\ge R.\n\\end{cases}\n}\n\\tag{A-08}\n\\]\n\nHere\n\n\\[\nr^2=x^2+y^2+z^2.\n\\]\n\nThe density and its first spatial derivatives vanish at \\(r=R\\). The profile is therefore \\(C^1\\), with no distributional surface delta arising from its first derivative.\n\nBecause the density is stationary,\n\n\\[\n\\partial_t\\rho=0.\n\\]\n\nThus\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\nabla_\\mu j^\\mu=0\n}\n\\]\n\nand\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{dJ=0.}\n\\]\n\nBut\n\n\\[\n*j^\\flat\\text{ is not the relevant expression;}\n\\qquad\n*J=j^\\flat=-\\rho\\,dt.\n\\]\n\nTherefore,\n\n\\[\nd(*J)=-d\\rho\\wedge dt.\n\\]\n\nAt\n\n\\[\nx=\\frac R2,\\qquad y=z=0,\n\\]\n\nthe symbolic calculation gives\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\nd(*J)=\n-\\frac{3}{2R}\\,dt\\wedge dx.\n}\n\\tag{A-09}\n\\]\n\nThis is nonzero.\n\n**A stationary, conserved, finite-core current fails the historical \\(d^*J=0\\) condition.**\n\nThat is a direct geometric discriminator, not an observational fit.\n\nIt shows why the distinction between the two differential operators matters when moving from historical worldlines to finite-core H(s)H worldtubes.\n\n---\n\n# 5. Topological control\n\nA separate test examined a normalized three-form on punctured Euclidean four-space.\n\nLet\n\n\\[\nX=(t,x,y,z),\n\\qquad\nr^2=t^2+x^2+y^2+z^2.\n\\]\n\nDefine\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\omega=\n\\frac{\\iota_X\\mathrm{vol}}{2\\pi^2r^4}.\n}\n\\tag{A-10}\n\\]\n\nOn\n\n\\[\n\\mathbb R^4\\setminus\\{0\\},\n\\]\n\nthe calculation gives\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{d\\omega=0.}\n\\]\n\nIts pullback to the unit three-sphere is\n\n\\[\n\\omega_{S^3}\n=\n\\frac{\n\\sin^2\\chi\\sin\\theta\n}{\n2\\pi^2\n}\n\\,d\\chi\\wedge d\\theta\\wedge d\\phi.\n\\]\n\nDirect integration gives\n\n\\[\n\\boxed{\n\\int_{S^3}\\omega=1.\n}\n\\tag{A-11}\n\\]\n\nThis demonstrates that a closed three-form need not be globally exact.\n\nThe calculation is a mathematical topology control in Euclidean space, not an additional physical assertion about Minkowski spacetime.\n\nIt also distinguishes local conservation from global questions about source topology.\n\n---\n\n# 6. Complete executed Python\n\nThe following code was executed with SymPy. It checks the general identity, Lorentzian Hodge signs, two counterexamples, finite-core profile and topological flux.\n\n**[Download executable audit](sandbox:/mnt/data/alberr_s4_20261020/three_form_audit.py)**\n\nSHA-256:\n\n`b2925eeb01c10ab51034bd5a42c64d996038a6d74c00f752be35aa8a4175f448`\n\n```python\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"CALC-S4-20261020T1248Z-ALBERR-01\nSource-anchored exterior-calculus test of historical 3-form current statement.\n\nRecovered (not silently edited):\n  J_{mu nu rho} ~ epsilon_{alpha mu nu rho} v^alpha\n  d^*J=0 => partial_mu J^mu=0\nSources: SAT H(s)H MANIFOLDS.txt, HsH F1 WHIRLIGIGG.txt.\n\nNew test uses standard Hodge * with diagonal Minkowski metric (-,+,+,+)\nand orientation dt^dx^dy^dz. For this test J=*j_flat=i_j(vol).\n\"\"\"\nimport itertools\nimport sympy as s\n\ncoords=s.symbols('t x y z',real=True)\nt,x,y,z=coords\nn=4\nbasis=(0,1,2,3)\nmetric=(-1,1,1,1)\n\ndef sign_perm(seq):\n    return (-1)**sum(seq[i]>seq[j] for i in range(len(seq)) for j in range(i+1,len(seq)))\n\ndef simp(A):\n    return {k:s.simplify(v) for k,v in A.items() if s.simplify(v)!=0}\n\ndef wedge(A,B):\n    out={}\n    for ia,a in A.items():\n        for ib,b in B.items():\n            if set(ia)&set(ib):continue\n            order=ia+ib\n            key=tuple(sorted(order))\n            out[key]=out.get(key,0)+a*b*sign_perm(order)\n    return simp(out)\n\ndef exterior(A):\n    out={}\n    for idx,val in A.items():\n        for i,q in enumerate(coords):\n            if i in idx:continue\n            term=wedge({(i,):s.diff(val,q)},{idx:s.S.One})\n            for key,v in term.items():out[key]=out.get(key,0)+v\n    return simp(out)\n\ndef star(A,g=metric):\n    out={}\n    for I,v in A.items():\n        J=tuple(i for i in basis if i not in I)\n        coeff=s.prod(g[i] for i in I)*sign_perm(I+J)\n        out[J]=out.get(J,0)+v*coeff\n    return simp(out)\n\ndef one_form_from_vector(j,g=metric):\n    return simp({(i,):g[i]*j[i] for i in basis})\n\ndef divergence(j):\n    return s.expand(sum(s.diff(j[i],coords[i]) for i in basis))\n\ndef current(j,g=metric):\n    return star(one_form_from_vector(j,g),g)\n\ndef eq_form(A,B):\n    keys=set(A)|set(B)\n    return all(s.simplify(A.get(k,0)-B.get(k,0))==0 for k in keys)\n\ndef fmt(A):\n    return ' + '.join(f'({s.sstr(v)})e{key}' for key,v in sorted(A.items())) or '0'\n\n# Star-star sign in signature (-+++): **=(-1)^(k(4-k)+1)\nfor k in range(5):\n    I=tuple(range(k))\n    f={I:s.S.One}\n    actual=star(star(f))\n    expected={I:s.Integer((-1)**(k*(4-k)+1))}\n    assert eq_form(actual,expected),(k,actual,expected)\n\n# General symbolic vector: dJ = (div j) vol, and *J=j_flat.\njt,jx,jy,jz=[s.Function(name)(*coords) for name in ('jt','jx','jy','jz')]\nj=(jt,jx,jy,jz)\njflat=one_form_from_vector(j)\nJ=current(j)\nassert
